   Click "Edit" tab (if need changes)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 5: COMMIT README TO GITHUB
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Scroll down in GitHub editor:

Commit message box:
   Type: "added README"

Click:
   "Commit new file"

What happens:
   ├─ README.md saved to GitHub
   ├─ Commit created on main branch
   ├─ File now visible on repo page
   └─ GitHub automatically merged (no manual merge needed)

CURRENT STATE:

LOCAL REPO (Your laptop):
   ├─ VPN.py ✓
   ├─ app.js ✓
   ├─ login.html ✓
   ├─ processing-modal.js ✓
   └─ README.md ✗ (NOT HERE YET)

GITHUB REPO (Cloud):
   ├─ VPN.py ✓
   ├─ app.js ✓
   ├─ login.html ✓
   ├─ processing-modal.js ✓
   └─ README.md ✓ (JUST CREATED)

STATUS: OUT OF SYNC ✗

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 6: IDENTIFY PROBLEM
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Problem:
   README.md exists on GitHub
   But NOT on your local machine

Risk:
   ├─ You continue work locally
   ├─ Make changes to VPN.py, app.js
   ├─ Push to GitHub
   ├─ Your old code (without README) overwrites
   ├─ README.md DELETED from GitHub ✗
   └─ LOST!

Solution:
   └─ Download latest code from GitHub first
   └─ Use: git pull command

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 7: PULL README TO LOCAL
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Open terminal/command prompt:
   $ cd /path/to/network-automation

Run:
   $ git pull origin main

Or (if upstream set):
   $ git pull

What it does:
   1. Connects to GitHub
   2. Downloads latest code (README.md)
   3. Merges into your local repo
   4. Updates your files

Terminal output:
   remote: Enumerating objects: 4, done.
   remote: Counting objects: 100% (4/4), done.
   Unpacking objects: 100% (4/4), done.
   From https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git
      abc123..def456  main -> origin/main
   Updating abc123..def456
   Fast-forward
    README.md | 20 ++
    1 file changed, 20 insertions(+)
    create mode 100644 README.md

Output meaning:
   ├─ remote: = Downloading from GitHub
   ├─ Unpacking = Extracting README.md
   ├─ Fast-forward = Clean merge (no conflicts)
   ├─ README.md created = File added locally
   └─ 20 insertions = 20 lines of content added

AFTER PULL STATE:

LOCAL REPO (Your laptop):
   ├─ VPN.py ✓
   ├─ app.js ✓
   ├─ login.html ✓
   ├─ processing-modal.js ✓
   └─ README.md ✓ (NOW HERE!)

GITHUB REPO (Cloud):
   ├─ VPN.py ✓
   ├─ app.js ✓
   ├─ login.html ✓
   ├─ processing-modal.js ✓
   └─ README.md ✓

STATUS: SYNCED ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 8: VERIFY README EXISTS LOCALLY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Check files:
   $ ls -la (Mac/Linux)
   $ dir (Windows)

You should see:
   README.md ✓

Open and read:
   $ cat README.md

Or in editor:
   (double click README.md)

Verify content:
   └─ See your markdown content
   └─ Headings, bullets, text all there ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 9: CONTINUE DEVELOPMENT SAFELY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Now safe to work:
   ├─ Make changes to VPN.py
   ├─ Update app.js
   ├─ Modify login.html
   └─ README.md won't get deleted ✓

Normal workflow:

Make changes:
   (Edit VPN.py or other files)

Commit:
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "added new VPN feature"

Push:
   $ git push origin main

Result:
   ├─ Your changes uploaded
   ├─ README.md still there ✓
   ├─ GitHub updated
   └─ Safe push ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHY GIT PULL IMPORTANT (Real scenarios)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Scenario 1: Created file on GitHub
   └─ Pull to get file locally

Scenario 2: Team member pushed code
   ├─ Their changes on GitHub
   ├─ Pull to get latest

Scenario 3: Worked from different machine
   ├─ That machine pushed updates
   ├─ Pull to sync

Scenario 4: Colleague modified files
   ├─ Changes on GitHub
   ├─ Pull before continuing

Best Practice:
   ALWAYS pull before starting work
   └─ Ensures latest code
   └─ Prevents overwriting
   └─ Safe collaboration

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PUSH vs PULL (Simple)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

PUSH (Upload):
   $ git push origin main
   YOUR laptop → GITHUB (share code)

PULL (Download):
   $ git pull origin main
   GITHUB → YOUR laptop (get latest code)

Safe order:
   1. PULL first (get latest)
   2. WORK (make changes)
   3. COMMIT (save locally)
   4. PUSH last (upload)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COMPLETE WORKFLOW SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Step 1: Initial push
   $ git push origin main
   (Your network-automation code on GitHub)

Step 2: Create README on GitHub
   (GitHub web editor)
   Commit "added README"

Step 3: Identify sync issue
   Local missing README.md
   GitHub has it

Step 4: Pull to sync
   $ git pull origin main
   README.md downloaded locally

Step 5: Verify sync
   $ ls (see README.md)

Step 6: Continue work safely
   (Make changes, commit, push)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
QUICK COMMANDS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Pull latest code:
   $ git pull origin main

Or:
   $ git pull

Check files:
   $ ls -la

View README:
   $ cat README.md

Check status:
   $ git status

Push changes:
   $ git push origin main

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
KEY POINTS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

✓ README.md documents project
✓ Create on GitHub or locally
✓ After creating on GitHub, pull to sync
✓ Pull = download latest from GitHub
✓ Always pull before pushing
✓ Prevents overwriting important files
✓ Keeps local & GitHub in sync

For your network-automation project:
✓ README explains VPN automation

28.CLONING REPOSITORIES - COMPLETE GUIDE
----------------------------------------
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT IS CLONING?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Cloning = Download entire repository from GitHub to local
   ├─ Gets all code files
   ├─ Gets all commit history
   ├─ Automatically links to remote
   └─ Ready to work immediately

Why clone?
   ├─ You want to use someone's code
   ├─ You want to contribute to project
   ├─ You want to work on code locally
   ├─ Different from downloading zip
   └─ Has automatic Git connection

Different from download:
   Download ZIP = Just files (no Git history)
   Clone = Full repo + Git connection + history

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
GITHUB TABS (Overview)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

On GitHub repo page, many tabs available:

Code (Main tab):
   └─ View & browse code files

Actions:
   └─ GitHub Actions (CI/CD automation)
   └─ Covered later in course

Issues:
   └─ Bug reports & feature requests
   └─ Covered later

Pull Requests:
   └─ Merge requests from contributors
   └─ Covered later

Projects:
   └─ Project management board
   └─ Organize tasks & milestones

Wiki:
   └─ Additional documentation
   └─ FAQs & help guides

Security:
   └─ Security settings & alerts
   └─ Vulnerability reports

Insights:
   └─ Analytics & statistics
   └─ Contribution graphs

Settings:
   └─ Repository configuration
   └─ Change visibility, name, rules

For this course:
   └─ Focus on Code, Actions, Issues, Pull Requests

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PUBLIC REPOSITORY (Anyone can view)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Public repos visible without login:
   ├─ Can view all code
   ├─ Can see all files
   ├─ Can browse history
   └─ Don't need GitHub account

As anonymous user:
   ├─ Can view code ✓
   ├─ Can clone repo ✓
   ├─ Cannot modify settings ✗
   ├─ Cannot push changes ✗
   └─ Cannot edit from GitHub web ✗

Settings tab:
   └─ Only visible if you own repo

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 1: VIEW PUBLIC REPOSITORY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Scenario:
   You found interesting project on GitHub
   Example: MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation
   You want to use or contribute

View without login:
   1. Go to github.com
   2. Search for repository
   3. View publicly available code
   4. No login needed

You see:
   ├─ All code files
   ├─ README.md
   ├─ Commit history
   ├─ Branches
   └─ All content

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 2: SETUP NEW GIT USER (Simulate different person)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Scenario in video:
   You are different person with different GitHub account
   Working on different machine
   Want to contribute to project

Setup new Git user locally:

Command:
   $ git config --global user.name "New Username"
   $ git config --global user.email "new-email@example.com"

What it does:
   ├─ Sets Git user on your system
   ├─ All commits use this name & email
   ├─ Links to GitHub account
   └─ Must match GitHub email

Example:
   $ git config --global user.name "john-dev"
   $ git config --global user.email "john@example.com"

Verify:
   $ git config --global --list
   (shows your Git config)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 3: CLONE REPOSITORY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

On GitHub repo page:
   Click "Code" button (green button)

Copy URL:
   ├─ HTTPS method (easiest)
   ├─ Copy URL with .git at end
   └─ Example: https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git

In terminal:
   $ git clone https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git

OR (with custom folder name):
   $ git clone https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git git-demo

What happens:
   1. Creates new folder (network-automation or git-demo)
   2. Downloads all code from GitHub
   3. Downloads all commit history
   4. Automatically links to remote (origin)
   5. Ready to work immediately

Download starts:
   Cloning into 'git-demo'...
   remote: Enumerating objects: 50, done.
   remote: Counting objects: 100% (50/50), done.
   Receiving objects: 100% (50/50), 123 KiB, done.
   Resolving deltas: 100% (25/25), done.

RESULT:

LOCAL FOLDER:
   git-demo/
   ├─ VPN.py
   ├─ app.js
   ├─ login.html
   ├─ processing-modal.js
   ├─ README.md
   ├─ .git folder (automatically created)
   ├─ .gitignore
   └─ (all other files)

Remote automatically set:
   └─ origin = github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 4: OPEN CLONED PROJECT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Open in editor:
   $ cd git-demo
   $ code . (if using VSCode)

Or manually:
   Open folder in VSCode/editor

In editor:
   ├─ See all files from repo
   ├─ README.md visible
   ├─ All code files present
   ├─ Git history available
   └─ Can edit & work

Note about .gitignore:
   └─ Files in .gitignore not cloned
   └─ Example: .vscode settings folder
   └─ These are local only (not in repo)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 5: VERIFY REMOTE CONNECTION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

In cloned project terminal:

Check remote:
   $ git remote

Output:
   origin

Get remote URL:
   $ git remote get-url origin

Output:
   https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git

What it means:
   ├─ origin = Remote reference created automatically
   ├─ URL = Linked to original repository
   ├─ No need to manually setup (unlike git remote add)
   └─ Automatic connection established

Important note:
   └─ Username NOT in URL
   └─ Because you're different user
   └─ Original creator's username not shown
   └─ Makes sense (you're new contributor)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHY CLONE IMPORTANT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Scenarios for cloning:

1. Contributing to open source:
   ├─ Find project on GitHub
   ├─ Clone to local
   ├─ Make improvements
   ├─ Push to your fork
   └─ Submit pull request

2. Using existing project:
   ├─ Clone someone's code
   ├─ Use as foundation
   ├─ Modify for your needs
   └─ Give credit to original

3. Team collaboration:
   ├─ Team member clones repo
   ├─ Works on features
   ├─ Pushes back to GitHub
   └─ Collaborates with others

4. Starting new development:
   ├─ Clone your own repo
   ├─ Work from different machine
   ├─ Continue development
   └─ Stay in sync

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
CLONE vs DOWNLOAD (Comparison)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

CLONE:
   $ git clone URL
   ├─ Gets full repository
   ├─ Includes .git folder
   ├─ Has commit history
   ├─ Automatic remote link
   ├─ Can push/pull
   ├─ Better for development
   └─ Recommended for Git projects

DOWNLOAD ZIP:
   Click "Download ZIP"
   ├─ Gets just files
   ├─ No .git folder
   ├─ No commit history
   ├─ No remote connection
   ├─ Can't push/pull
   ├─ Better for quick view
   └─ Not for development

Use clone for:
   └─ Contributing to projects
   └─ Working with Git repos

Use download for:
   └─ Quick file access only
   └─ No Git needed

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
CLONE COMMAND OPTIONS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Basic clone (uses repo name):
   $ git clone https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git

   Creates: network-automation/ folder

Clone with custom name:
   $ git clone https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git my-vp-project

   Creates: my-vp-project/ folder

Clone specific branch:
   $ git clone -b feature-branch https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git

   Clones only specified branch

Clone to specific path:
   $ git clone https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git /path/to/folder

   Clones to given path

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
TYPICAL WORKFLOW AFTER CLONE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Step 1: Clone repository
   $ git clone URL

Step 2: Enter cloned folder
   $ cd git-demo

Step 3: Verify remote
   $ git remote -v

Step 4: Pull latest (optional but good practice)
   $ git pull origin main

Step 5: Create feature branch
   $ git checkout -b my-feature

Step 6: Make changes
   (Edit files)

Step 7: Commit changes
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "added feature"

Step 8: Push to your fork (if contributing)
   $ git push origin my-feature

Step 9: Create Pull Request on GitHub
   (Request to merge into original repo)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
CLONED vs ORIGINAL REPO
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

ORIGINAL REPO (MANISH-GANVIR):
   └─ github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation
   ├─ Owner can push directly
   ├─ Can merge pull requests
   ├─ Can change settings
   └─ Main project

CLONED REPO (Different user on local):
   └─ Your local folder git-demo/
   ├─ You can read all code
   ├─ You can make changes locally
   ├─ Can't push directly (not owner)
   ├─ Would need to fork & PR
   └─ Your copy for development

Connection:
   └─ origin = points to MANISH-GANVIR's repo
   └─ You can pull latest
   └─ You can't push (permission)
   └─ Would fork first for contribution

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
.GITIGNORE FILES (Not cloned)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Files in .gitignore are NOT cloned:

Example:
   .vscode/ (folder)
   node_modules/ (folder)
   .env (file)
   *.log (files)

Why not cloned?
   ├─ Local only files
   ├─ User-specific settings
   ├─ Generated files
   ├─ Sensitive data (passwords)
   └─ Not needed by others

When you clone:
   └─ These folders missing
   └─ That's expected
   └─ Create your own if needed

Example in video:
   └─ .vscode folder not cloned
   └─ Different machines need different settings

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
QUICK COMMANDS SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Clone repository:
   $ git clone URL

Clone with custom folder:
   $ git clone URL folder-name

Check remote:
   $ git remote

Get remote URL:
   $ git remote get-url origin

List all remotes:
   $ git remote -v

Enter cloned folder:
   $ cd folder-name

View files:
   $ ls

Check status:
   $ git status

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
KEY POINTS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━���━━━━━━

✓ Clone = Download entire repo with history
✓ Different from ZIP download
✓ Automatic remote connection
✓ Works with public repos (no login needed)
✓ Can clone without owning repo
✓ .gitignore files not cloned
✓ Ready to work immediately
✓ Can pull, but can't push (unless owner)
✓ Perfect for open source contribution
✓ Simulates working on different machine
=================================================
29. Making Changes As A Different User (Non-Owner)
-------------------------------------------------
CONTRIBUTING TO OTHERS' REPOS - REALISTIC EXAMPLE

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FLOW SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Find project → Fork → Clone your fork → Make changes →
Commit → Push → Create PR → Wait for review → Merged! ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
REAL SCENARIO
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Situation:
   You're scrolling GitHub one day
   Find cool VPN automation project
   It's MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation
   You notice: Title color is ugly (blue)
   Think: "I can improve this!"

Decision: Contribute to the project

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PROBLEM: PERMISSION DENIED
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

What you tried:
   Downloaded repo (cloned it)
   Changed styles.css (color: orange)
   Committed locally
   Tried to push

Terminal:
   $ git push origin feat-color

   Error:
   remote: Permission to MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git
   denied to your-username.

Reality check:
   └─ "I don't own this repo, so I can't push directly"

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
REAL SOLUTION: FORK FIRST
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Think of it like this:
   Original book: Someone else's
   Fork: You make your own copy
   You edit your copy: No problem
   You show original owner: "Check out my improvement!"
   Owner likes it: "Merge it in!"

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 1: GO TO REPO & FORK
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

You're on: github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation

See the repo page:
   ├─ Code files listed
   ├─ README.md visible
   ├─ Stars count shown
   └─ Top right: Buttons (Star, Watch, Fork)

Click: "Fork" button

What happens:
   GitHub processing...
   Redirects to: github.com/your-username/network-automation

You see:
   "Forked from MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation"
   └─ Shows it's your copy of original

Now you own a copy in your account!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 2: CLONE YOUR FORK (Not original!)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

You're on your fork page now

Click: Green "Code" button
Copy: HTTPS URL (with .git)

On your laptop, open terminal:
   $ cd Documents/projects

Clone YOUR fork:
   $ git clone https://github.com/your-username/network-automation.git

Terminal output:
   Cloning into 'network-automation'...
   remote: Enumerating objects: 45, done.
   remote: Counting objects: 100% (45/45), done.
   Receiving objects: 100% (45/45), 234 KiB | 512 KiB/s, done.
   Resolving deltas: 100% (18/18), done.

Move into folder:
   $ cd network-automation

Check what's there:
   $ ls

   Output:
   VPN.py
   app.js
   login.html
   styles.css
   README.md
   ...

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 3: CREATE WORKING BRANCH
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

You're on main branch currently

Create new branch for your change:
   $ git checkout -b feat-color

Output:
   Switched to a new branch 'feat-color'

Verify:
   $ git branch

   Output:
   * feat-color
     main

(asterisk shows current branch)

Why separate branch?
   └─ Keep main clean
   └─ Easy to manage
   └─ Professional practice

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 4: ACTUALLY MAKE THE CHANGE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Open styles.css in your editor (VSCode, etc.)

Find title styling:
   .title {
     color: blue;
     font-size: 24px;
   }

Change it:
   .title {
     color: #FFA500;  ← Changed to orange
     font-size: 24px;
   }

Save file: Ctrl+S (or Cmd+S)

Check what changed:
   $ git status

   Output:
   On branch feat-color

   Changes not staged for commit:
     modified:   styles.css

See the actual change:
   $ git diff styles.css

   Output:
   - color: blue;
   + color: #FFA500;

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 5: SAVE YOUR WORK LOCALLY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Stage the file:
   $ git add styles.css

Or add everything:
   $ git add .

Check staged:
   $ git status

   Output:
   On branch feat-color

   Changes to be committed:
     modified:   styles.css

Commit with clear message:
   $ git commit -m "changed title color from blue to orange for better UI"

Output:
   [feat-color a1b2c3d] changed title color from blue to orange...
    1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)

Reality:
   └─ Your change now saved locally
   └─ Only on your laptop right now
   └─ Not on GitHub yet

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 6: PUSH TO YOUR FORK ON GITHUB
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Push your branch:
   $ git push origin feat-color

Output:
   Enumerating objects: 3, done.
   Counting objects: 100% (3/3), done.
   Writing objects: 100% (1/1), 234 bytes, done.
   remote: Create a pull request for 'feat-color' on GitHub by visiting:
   remote: https://github.com/your-username/network-automation/pull/new/feat-color
   To https://github.com/your-username/network-automation.git
    * [new branch]      feat-color -> feat-color

Reality:
   └─ NO PERMISSION ERROR! ✓
   └─ You own your fork
   └─ Push worked!
   └─ Changes now on GitHub

Refresh your fork on GitHub:
   Go to: github.com/your-username/network-automation

You see:
   ├─ Branch dropdown shows "feat-color"
   ├─ Yellow notification: "Compare & pull request"
   └─ Your changes visible in feat-color branch

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 7: CREATE PULL REQUEST
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

On your fork page:
   Click: Yellow "Compare & pull request" button

What opens:
   PR creation page

   Shows:
   ├─ feat-color → main (your changes going to main)
   ├─ "Create pull request" button
   └─ Title field (auto-filled)

Edit title:
   Current: "changed title color from blue to orange..."
   (Usually auto-filled from your commit message)

Add description:
   "Improved the UI by changing the title color to orange.
    The blue color was too plain.
    Orange looks more modern and appealing."

Review changes:
   ├─ Click "Files changed" tab
   ├─ See green "+" (orange color added)
   ├─ See red "-" (blue color removed)
   └─ Verify looks correct

Click: "Create pull request"

Reality:
   └─ You just submitted your improvement!
   └─ MANISH-GANVIR gets notification
   └─ Original owner reviews your work
   └─ On their PR page now

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 8: WAIT & WATCH
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Real scenarios:

Scenario 1: Owner likes it
   ├─ Leaves comment: "Nice improvement!"
   ├─ Clicks "Merge pull request"
   ├─ Your code now in original repo ✓
   ├─ You're a contributor! 🎉
   └─ Your name in commit history

Scenario 2: Owner wants changes
   ├─ Comments: "Can you use a different orange?"
   ├─ You go back to your laptop
   ├─ Edit styles.css again
   ├─ Push again: $ git push origin feat-color
   ├─ PR updates automatically
   └─ Owner reviews again

Scenario 3: Owner declines
   ├─ Comments: "We like the blue"
   ├─ Closes PR
   ├─ Your code stays in your fork
   ├─ Not merged to original
   └─ No worries, you tried!

Real timeline:
   ├─ Quick repos: Response in hours
   ├─ Popular repos: Days or weeks
   ├─ Busy maintainers: Could be months
   └─ Just be patient

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT ACTUALLY HAPPENED
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Before:
   MANISH-GANVIR owns: network-automation repo
   You: Just a visitor (read-only)

After fork:
   You own: your-username/network-automation
   MANISH-GANVIR owns: MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation (unchanged)

After push:
   Your fork has: Your improvements
   Original still has: Original code

After PR merged (if approved):
   Original repo has: Your improvements too!
   You're now: A contributor

Real impact:
   └─ Project got better
   └─ Open source works!
   └─ Anyone can improve anything
   └─ Democracy of code

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
IMPORTANT: WHY FORK?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━���━━━━━━━

Don't fork = Permission denied
   └─ Can't push to someone else's repo

Fork = You own copy
   ├─ Can push to your copy
   ├─ Can submit improvements
   └─ Original owner can review & merge

Real world:
   └─ GitHub forces this workflow
   └─ Protects original projects
   └─ Allows anyone to contribute
   └─ Professional standard

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
QUICK COMMAND CHEAT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Fork (GitHub web):
   Click "Fork" button

Clone your fork:
   $ git clone https://github.com/your-username/repo.git
   $ cd repo

Branch & code:
   $ git checkout -b feat-color
   (Edit styles.css)

Commit & push:
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "changed color"
   $ git push origin feat-color

PR (GitHub web):
   Click "Compare & pull request"
   Submit PR
============================================================
30.GitHub Issues & Collaborators:-
----------------------------------
GETTING CHANGES INTO OTHERS' REPOS

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PROBLEM
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Situation:
   You cloned someone's repo (MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation)
   Made changes locally (changed title color)
   Committed changes
   Want to push to GitHub

When you try:
   $ git push origin feat-color

Error:
   remote: Permission to MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git
   denied to your-username.

Why error?
   └─ You don't own the repo
   └─ You don't have push permission
   └─ Only repo owner can push directly

Reality:
   └─ How do you get your changes into original repo?

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SOLUTION - TWO OPTIONS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Option 1: FORK (Recommended, easiest)
   └─ Already covered before

Option 2: ASK PERMISSION (Alternative)
   └─ Create issue → Owner adds you → Push → PR

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 1: CREATE ISSUE WITH SUGGESTION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Go to: github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation

Click: "Issues" tab

See:
   ├─ List of existing issues
   └─ "New issue" button

Click: "New issue"

What opens:
   Issue creation form

Fill Title:
   "Suggestion: Change Title Color"

Fill Description:
   "I think title looks better in orange.

    ```css
    .title {
      color: #FFA500;
    }
    ```

    What do you think?"

Why this approach?
   ├─ Contact repo owner
   ├─ Show your idea
   ├─ Give code suggestion
   ├─ Professional way to communicate
   └─ Issues = feature for this

Submit:
   Click "Submit new issue"

Result:
   Your suggestion posted
   Owner gets notification

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 2: WAIT FOR OWNER RESPONSE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Owner can do 3 things:

Option A: Implement themselves
   Owner reads issue
   Owner implements change
   Your idea made it in! ✓
   You don't need push access

Option B: Ignores issue
   Owner doesn't like suggestion
   Closes issue
   Nothing happens
   You tried though!

Option C: Asks you to do it
   Owner comments: "Can you implement this?"
   Owner trusts you
   Wants you to push directly
   Go to Step 3

Real scenario in demo: Option C

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 3: OWNER ADDS YOU AS COLLABORATOR
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Owner's action (he does this):
   Go to repo settings
   Click: "Collaborators"
   Click: "Add people"
   Type: Your GitHub username
   Click: "Add collaborator"
   GitHub sends invitation

You receive:
   Email invitation
   Subject: "Added as collaborator"
   Contains: Invitation link

You accept:
   Click link in email
   Accept invitation

Result:
   You now have push access! ✓

What you can do now:
   ├─ Push to branches ✓
   ├─ Create branches ✓
   ├─ Can't push directly to main (protected)
   └─ Can create PR to main

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 4: MAKE CHANGES LOCALLY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

On your laptop:

Create branch:
   $ git checkout -b feat-color

Edit file:
   styles.css: change color from blue to orange

Check changes:
   $ git status
   $ git diff styles.css

Stage:
   $ git add .

Commit:
   $ git commit -m "changed title color from blue to orange"

What you have:
   ├─ Local changes on feat-color branch
   ├─ Committed locally
   ├─ Ready to push
   └─ Not on GitHub yet

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 5: PUSH TO ORIGINAL REPO
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Push command:
   $ git push origin feat-color

Terminal asks:
   Username: (your GitHub username)
   Password: (personal access token)

Authentication:
   Copy your PAT (personal access token)
   Paste when prompted
   GitHub verifies you're collaborator

Output:
   Enumerating objects: 3, done.
   Counting objects: 100% (3/3), done.
   Writing objects: 100% (1/1), 234 bytes, done.
   remote: Create a pull request for 'feat-color' on GitHub
   To https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git
    * [new branch]      feat-color -> feat-color

Result:
   SUCCESS! No permission error! ✓
   Your branch on original repo
   Ready for next step

Why no error?
   ├─ You're collaborator
   ├─ Owner gave permission
   ├─ GitHub allows push
   └─ Different from before!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 6: OWNER SETS UP BRANCH PROTECTION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Owner wants quality control:
   Settings → Branches
   Click: "Add rule"

Configure:
   Branch: main
   Check: "Require pull request before merging"
   Save

What this does:
   ├─ No one can push directly to main
   ├─ Even you can't
   ├─ Must go through PR instead
   ├─ Forces code review
   └─ Protects production

Effect:
   You can push to feat-color ✓
   But can't push to main ✗
   Must create PR ✓

Why important?
   ├─ Main = production code
   ├─ Keep it stable
   ├─ Need review before changes
   ├─ Professional practice
   └─ Prevents accidents

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 7: CREATE PULL REQUEST
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

You have feat-color pushed
But can't go directly to main (protected)

Solution: Create PR

On GitHub:
   Go to repo
   Click: "Pull requests" tab
   Click: "New pull request"

Select branches:
   Base: main
   Compare: feat-color

Review:
   See your changes
   See files modified
   See commits

Add description:
   "Changed title color from blue to orange
    for better UI appearance"

Submit:
   Click: "Create pull request"

Result:
   PR created
   Owner gets notification
   Can see your changes
   Ready for review

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 8: OWNER REVIEWS & MERGES
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Owner sees:
   PR notification
   Can review code
   Can see changes
   Can leave comments

Owner's options:

Option 1: Approve & Merge
   Comments: "Looks great!"
   Clicks: "Merge pull request"
   Result: feat-color merged to main ✓

Option 2: Request changes
   Comments: "Can you use different orange?"
   You edit, commit, push again
   PR updates automatically
   Owner reviews again

Option 3: Reject PR
   Closes PR
   Changes not merged

Most likely: Option 1

Final result:
   ├─ feat-color merged to main ✓
   ├─ Your code in production ✓
   ├─ Issue closed ✓
   ├─ You're a contributor! 🎉
   └─ Your name in commit history

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
KEY CONCEPTS EXPLAINED
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

COLLABORATOR:
   Person added to repo
   Has push access to branches
   Can't push to protected main (usually)
   Member of project team

BRANCH PROTECTION:
   Rules on specific branches
   Common on main/master
   Require PR before merge
   Require code review
   Protect production code

PERSONAL ACCESS TOKEN:
   Password for Git operations
   More secure than GitHub password
   Can be revoked
   Used for authentication when pushing

PULL REQUEST:
   Formal way to propose changes
   Owner reviews before merging
   Discussion happens here
   Professional standard
   Protects main branch

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COMPARISON: FORK vs COLLABORATOR
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

FORK:
   ├─ No permission needed
   ├─ Click "Fork" button
   ├─ You own your copy
   ├─ Push to your fork ✓
   ├─ Submit PR for review
   ├─ Good for strangers
   ├─ Industry standard (open source)
   └─ Multiple forks allowed

COLLABORATOR:
   ├─ Owner must invite you
   ├─ Owner adds username
   ├─ Push to original repo ✓
   ├─ Usually still PR to main
   ├─ For trusted team members
   ├─ Internal projects
   └─ Only owner controls access

When to use FORK:
   └─ Contributing to open source project
   └─ Don't know the owner personally
   └─ Want independent copy
   └─ Don't want to ask permission

When to use COLLABORATOR:
   └─ Working on team project
   └─ Owner explicitly invites you
   └─ Working on internal project
   └─ More convenient than fork
   └─ Direct access to original

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
QUICK COMMANDS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━���━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Create issue:
   (GitHub web - Issues - New issue)

Accept collaborator invite:
   (Email link)

Create & work on branch:
   $ git checkout -b feat-color
   (Edit files)
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "changed color"

Push:
   $ git push origin feat-color
   Username: your-username
   Password: (paste PAT)

Create PR:
   (GitHub web - Pull requests - New)
==============================================================
31. Working with Pull Requests
--------------------------------
PULL REQUESTS & MERGING ON GITHUB - COMPLETE

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PROBLEM
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

You pushed feat-color branch

What does "pushed" mean?
   "Pushed" = Upload to GitHub
   "feat-color branch" = Feature branch (your changes)

   Together:
      You uploaded your changes to GitHub

Details:
   ├─ Your laptop had: feat-color branch with changes
   ├─ You ran: $ git push origin feat-color
   ├─ GitHub now has: feat-color branch with changes
   └─ Your work backed up & visible to team

You want to merge it into main
But can't push directly to main (protected)

How to merge?
   └─ Use Pull Request (PR)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT IS PULL REQUEST:Formal request to merge your branch into main
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PR = Pull Request
   = Formal request to merge your branch into main
   = Owner reviews your code before merging
   = Professional way to add code to project

Example:
   You: "I made changes, can you review & merge?"
   Owner: "Let me check... Looks good! Merging!"
   Result: Your code in main ✓

Real world example:
   You: "Hey, can you merge my branch?"
   Owner: "Let me review first"
   Owner: "Looks good, merging!"
   Done! ✓

Steps:
   You create PR
   Owner reviews code
   Owner merges
   Your code in main

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PUSHING FEAT-COLOR BRANCH - DETAILED
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

What "Pushed" means:
   "Pushed" = Upload to GitHub
   "feat-color branch" = Feature branch (your changes)

   Together:
      You uploaded your changes to GitHub

Step by step:

Step 1: Created branch locally
   $ git checkout -b feat-color
   └─ Branch exists on your laptop only

Step 2: Made changes
   Edit styles.css (color: blue → orange)
   Save file

Step 3: Committed
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "changed color"
   └─ Changes saved in feat-color branch locally

Step 4: Pushed
   $ git push origin feat-color
   └─ Uploads feat-color branch to GitHub

Before push:
   YOUR LAPTOP:
   ├─ main branch
   └─ feat-color branch (with changes)

   GITHUB:
   ├─ main branch only
   └─ feat-color doesn't exist yet

After push:
   YOUR LAPTOP:
   ├─ main branch
   └─ feat-color branch (with changes)

   GITHUB:
   ├─ main branch
   └─ feat-color branch (with changes) ✓

Result of pushing:
   ├─ Your changes backed up on GitHub
   ├─ Team can see your work
   ├─ Ready for code review
   └─ Ready to create PR

After push, GitHub shows:
   ├─ feat-color branch exists
   ├─ Shows your commits
   ├─ Shows exact changes (blue → orange)
   ├─ Button: "Compare & pull request"
   └─ Ready for code review

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 1: CREATE PULL REQUEST
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Your feat-color branch is on GitHub
Now create PR to merge it into main

On GitHub:
   Go to: Pull requests tab
   Click: New pull request

Select:
   Base: main (target - where to merge into)
   Compare: feat-color (source - what to merge)

Click: Create pull request

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 2: ADD TITLE & DESCRIPTION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Title:
   "Changed title color to orange"

Description:
   "I changed the color as suggested in issue #1
    This looks much better"

Link issue:
   Type: #1
   (Closes issue when PR merges)

Click: Create pull request

What happens:
   ├─ PR created
   ├─ Owner gets notification
   ├─ Can't merge yet (needs review)
   └─ Waiting for owner

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 3: OWNER REVIEWS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Owner gets notification
Owner reads PR:
   ├─ Title: "Changed title color to orange"
   ├─ Description: Why you made changes
   ├─ Files changed: See diff (blue → orange)
   ├─ Commits: Review all changes
   └─ Issue linked: See context

Owner can:
   ├─ Leave comments
   ├─ Request changes
   └─ Approve

In this case: Owner approves

Owner clicks: Review changes → Approve

Status: "Approved" ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 3.5: OWNER ASKS FOR CHANGES (ALTERNATE)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

What if owner doesn't like something?

Owner can:
   ├─ Leave comment on specific line
   ├─ Example: "Use darker orange, #FF8C00 instead"
   ├─ Click: Review changes → Request changes
   └─ Submit review

Status shows:
   "Changes requested" ⚠️

What you (creator) do:

Step 1: Read owner's feedback
   Go to PR page
   Read comments
   Understand what needs to change

Step 2: Make changes locally
   $ git checkout feat-color
   (Edit styles.css again)
   Change: color: #FFA500 → color: #FF8C00
   Save file

Step 3: Commit changes
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "used darker orange as suggested"

Step 4: Push again
   $ git push origin feat-color

   GitHub automatically updates PR! ✓

What happens:
   ├─ New commit appears in PR
   ├─ PR shows latest changes
   ├─ Owner gets notification
   ├─ Owner reviews again
   ├─ Owner approves
   └─ Ready to merge

Important:
   └─ You don't create new PR
   └─ Just push to same branch
   └─ Existing PR updates automatically

Real workflow:

Owner comments: "Use darker orange"

YOU:
   $ git checkout feat-color
   (Edit: #FFA500 → #FF8C00)
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "used darker orange"
   $ git push origin feat-color

PR updates automatically ✓

OWNER:
   Sees new commit
   Reviews again
   Approves ✓
   Merges ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 4: OWNER MERGES
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Now approved & can merge

Owner clicks: Merge pull request

GitHub shows merge options:

   Option 1: Create a merge commit
   Option 2: Squash and merge
   Option 3: Rebase and merge

What each means:

OPTION 1: CREATE MERGE COMMIT
   What it does:
      Merges branch as-is
      Creates merge commit
      Keeps all commits visible

   Command (if local):
      $ git merge feat-color

   Good for:
      Important features
      Want to see history
      Professional projects

OPTION 2: SQUASH AND MERGE
   What it does:
      Combines all commits into 1
      Cleaner history
      Main has 1 commit instead of multiple

   Example:
      feat-color had 3 commits
      After squash: 1 commit in main

   Good for:
      Many small commits
      Want clean history
      Feature branch work

OPTION 3: REBASE AND MERGE
   What it does:
      Replays commits on top of main
      Linear history
      No merge commit

   Good for:
      Clean, linear history
      Avoid merge commits
      Advanced teams

For this example: Choose OPTION 1 (default)

Owner selects: Create a merge commit
Owner clicks: Confirm merge

What happens:
   ├─ feat-color merged to main ✓
   ├─ Merge commit created
   ├─ PR closed
   ├─ Issue #1 auto-closed (linked)
   └─ Code now in production ✓

Result:
   GITHUB MAIN:
   ├─ Includes your color change ✓
   └─ Merge commit visible

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 5: PULL LOCALLY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Changes on GitHub now
Not on your laptop yet

Sync with GitHub:
   $ git pull origin main

What happens:
   ├─ Downloads merge commit
   ├─ Updates your local main
   ├─ feat-color now in your main
   └─ Color change visible locally ✓

Both you & owner:
   $ git pull origin main

   Everyone has latest code
   Everyone synced! ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 6: DELETE BRANCH (OPTIONAL BUT RECOMMENDED)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

After merging:
   feat-color branch is now in main
   No longer needed
   Should clean up

GitHub automatically shows:
   "Delete branch" button
   (After merge completes)

Option 1: Delete on GitHub (easiest)
   Click: "Delete branch" button on PR
   Branch deleted from GitHub

Option 2: Delete locally on your laptop
   $ git branch -d feat-color

   Deletes local branch

Option 3: Delete from GitHub (manual)
   Go to: Branches tab
   Find: feat-color
   Click: Trash icon
   Deleted ✓

Why delete?
   ├─ Keep repo clean
   ├─ Less clutter
   ├─ Don't confuse old branches
   ├─ Professional practice
   └─ Code already in main

Result:
   GITHUB:
   ├─ main branch (has your changes)
   └─ feat-color deleted ✓

   YOUR LAPTOP:
   ├─ main branch (up to date)
   └─ feat-color deleted ✓

After cleanup:
   $ git branch

   Output:
   * main

   (Only main shown, feat-color gone)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
MERGE CONFLICTS - WHEN THEY HAPPEN
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

What is merge conflict?

Scenario:
   Person A: Edits line 5 of styles.css
   Person B: Also edits line 5 of styles.css
   Both pushed branches
   GitHub can't auto-merge (conflict!)

When it happens:
   ├─ You and owner edit same file, same line
   ├─ GitHub tries to merge, but can't decide
   ├─ Shows "Conflict" status on PR
   └─ Needs manual fix

GitHub shows:
   "This branch has conflicts that must be resolved"

How to fix:

Option 1: Resolve on GitHub (web editor)
   Click: "Resolve conflicts" button
   GitHub shows both versions
   You choose which version to keep
   Mark resolved
   Commit

Option 2: Resolve locally (command line)
   Pull latest main:
      $ git pull origin main

   Git shows conflicts:
      <<<<<<< HEAD
      color: blue;
      =======
      color: orange;
      >>>>>>> feat-color

   Edit file manually, choose one:
      color: orange;

   Stage & commit:
      $ git add .
      $ git commit -m "resolved conflict"

   Push:
      $ git push origin feat-color

   PR updates, conflict resolved ✓

How to avoid conflicts:
   ├─ Communicate with team
   ├─ Don't edit same files
   ├─ Merge main into your branch often
   ├─ Pull before making changes
   └─ Use branches properly

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COMPLETE WORKFLOW EXAMPLE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

YOU (Collaborator):
   $ git checkout -b feat-color
   (Edit styles.css - blue → orange)
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "changed color"
   $ git push origin feat-color
   └─ Branch now on GitHub ✓

GitHub shows:
   "Compare & pull request" button

YOU:
   Click: New pull request
   Title: "Changed title color to orange"
   Description: "Changes suggested in #1"
   Click: Create PR

OWNER:
   Gets notification
   Reviews code
   Sees color change

SCENARIO A - Owner approves:
   Owner: "Looks good!"
   Owner: Approve PR
   Owner: Merge PR

   RESULT:
   ├─ PR closed ✓
   ├─ Code in main ✓
   ├─ Issue #1 closed ✓
   └─ Delete branch ✓

SCENARIO B - Owner asks for changes:
   Owner: "Use darker orange"
   Owner: Request changes

   YOU:
   ├─ Read feedback
   ├─ Edit styles.css (darker orange)
   ├─ $ git add .
   ├─ $ git commit -m "used darker orange"
   ├─ $ git push origin feat-color
   └─ PR auto-updates ✓

   OWNER:
   ├─ Reviews again
   ├─ Approves
   └─ Merges PR

   RESULT:
   ├─ PR closed ✓
   ├─ Code in main ✓
   ├─ Delete branch ✓

FINAL STEP (both scenarios):

YOU & OWNER:
   $ git pull origin main
   Everyone synced ✓

YOU (cleanup):
   $ git branch -d feat-color
   Branch deleted locally ✓

Result:
   ├─ Code improved ✓
   ├─ In production ✓
   ├─ Everyone synced ✓
   ├─ Branch cleaned up ✓
   └─ Collaboration successful! 🎉

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHY PULL REQUEST?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Instead of direct push to main:

Benefits:
   ├─ Code review before merging
   ├─ Quality check
   ├─ Catch bugs early
   ├─ Discussion & feedback
   ├─ Main branch stays stable
   ├─ Iterate if needed
   └─ Professional standard

Why protected main?
   ├─ Production code
   ├─ Can't break it with direct push
   ├─ Forces code review
   ├─ Team safety
   └─ Industry practice

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
QUICK SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

1. Create branch locally
2. Make changes
3. Commit changes
4. Push branch to GitHub
   └─ "Pushed" = uploaded your changes ✓
5. Create PR (feat-color → main)
6. Owner reviews PR
7. Owner can:
   ├─ Approve (go to 8)
   ├─ Request changes (you edit & push again)
   └─ Reject (PR closed)
8. Owner merges PR
   └─ Choose merge strategy
9. Delete branch (cleanup)
10. Pull locally
    └─ Everyone synced ✓
11. Done! ✓
=============================================================
32. Understanding & Using Forks:-
--------------------------------
FORKING & PULL REQUESTS ACROSS REPOS

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PROBLEM
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Owner removed you as collaborator
You want to contribute to open source project
Can't push (no permission)
Don't want to ask for permission

How to contribute?
   └─ Fork the repository

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHY FORK INSTEAD OF COLLABORATOR?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Collaborator approach:
   ├─ Ask owner for permission
   ├─ Owner adds you
   ├─ You get push access
   └─ Risk: Owner trusts everyone

Problem:
   └─ Owners won't add random people
   └─ Security risk
   └─ Not practical for open source

Fork approach:
   ├─ No permission needed
   ├─ You own your copy
   ├─ You can push to YOUR copy
   ├─ Submit PR from your copy
   └─ Original owner reviews & decides

Better for:
   ├─ Open source projects
   ├─ Strangers contributing
   ├─ Unknown people
   ├─ Professional standard
   └─ Industry practice

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT IS FORK?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Fork = Copy repository to your GitHub account

Real world analogy:
   Original recipe: Someone's secret formula
   Fork: You copy the recipe
   Your copy: You own it, can modify it
   Original: Still unchanged
   Share your version: Submit to original owner

GitHub analogy:
   Original repo: MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation
   Fork: your-username/network-automation (YOUR COPY)
   Both exist independently
   Can submit PR from your copy to original

Result:
   ├─ You own your fork
   ├─ You can push to your fork
   ├─ Original owner reviews your PR
   └─ If approved, merged into original

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━��━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 1: FORK THE REPOSITORY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Go to original repo:
   github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation

Click: "Fork" button (top right)

GitHub shows:
   "Create a new fork"
   Repository name: network-automation (can change)
   Description: (optional)

Click: "Create fork"

What happens:
   GitHub creates copy in your account
   Redirects to: github.com/your-username/network-automation
   Shows: "Forked from MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation"

Result:
   YOUR FORK:
   ├─ your-username/network-automation
   ├─ You own it
   ├─ Same code as original
   └─ Independent copy

ORIGINAL REPO:
   ├─ MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation
   ├─ Unchanged
   └─ Still exists

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 2: CLONE YOUR FORK LOCALLY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Get your fork URL:
   Go to your fork page
   Click: Code button
   Copy: HTTPS URL

On your laptop:
   $ git clone https://github.com/your-username/network-automation.git git-demo-2

Navigate:
   $ cd git-demo-2

Verify remote:
   $ git remote -v

   Output:
   origin  https://github.com/your-username/network-automation.git (fetch)
   origin  https://github.com/your-username/network-automation.git (push)

Important:
   └─ origin points to YOUR FORK (you own it)
   └─ Not original repo
   └─ This allows you to push!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 3: CREATE FEATURE BRANCH & MAKE CHANGES
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Create branch:
   $ git checkout -b feat-link-color

Make useful changes:
   Edit styles.css
   Change link color: color: blue → color: #FF6B6B (reddish)
   Save file

Check status:
   $ git status

Important trick:
   You can create branch AFTER making changes!
   Just make changes first
   Then: $ git checkout -b feat-link-color
   Branch created with uncommitted changes

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 4: COMMIT & PUSH TO YOUR FORK
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Stage changes:
   $ git add styles.css

Commit:
   $ git commit -m "changed link color to reddish"

Push to YOUR fork:
   $ git push origin feat-link-color

Important:
   └─ You own your fork
   └─ Push succeeds! ✓
   └─ No permission error

Output:
   [new branch]      feat-link-color -> feat-link-color

Result:
   YOUR FORK on GitHub:
   ├─ main branch
   └─ feat-link-color branch ✓ (new!)

   ORIGINAL REPO:
   ├─ No change
   └─ Your branch doesn't exist there

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 5: CREATE PR ACROSS REPOS (KEY DIFFERENCE!)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Go to ORIGINAL repo:
   github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation

Click: "Pull requests" tab

Click: "New pull request"

Important: Select "compare across forks" option
   (This allows merging from different repo)

Select target & source:

Base repository:
   MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation (original)

Base branch:
   main

Compare (source):
   your-username/network-automation (your fork) ← Different repo!

Compare branch:
   feat-link-color

Click: Create pull request

What happens:
   PR created from YOUR FORK
   To ORIGINAL REPO
   Owner can review
   Owner can merge

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 6: ADD TITLE & DESCRIPTION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Title:
   "Changed link color to reddish"

Description:
   "Updated link styling for better appearance.
    Changed from blue to red (#FF6B6B).
    Looks much nicer!"

Click: Create pull request

Result:
   PR created ✓
   Owner gets notification
   Reviewer required
   Waiting for approval

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 7: OWNER REVIEWS & APPROVES
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Owner (original repo):
   Receives PR notification
   Reviews code
   Sees link color change
   Likes the improvement

Owner actions:
   Add yourself as reviewer
   Review changes
   Click: Approve
   Submit review

Status: "Approved" ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 8: OWNER MERGES
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Owner clicks: Merge pull request

Choose merge strategy:
   └─ Create merge commit (default)

Click: Confirm merge

What happens:
   feat-link-color from YOUR FORK
   Merged into ORIGINAL REPO main ✓
   PR closed
   Code in production ✓

Important realization:
   Code from YOUR FORK
   Now in ORIGINAL REPO
   You contributed! ✓
   You don't own original repo
   But your code is there

Result:

ORIGINAL REPO:
├─ main branch now has link color change ✓
└─ Forked from user contribution

YOUR FORK:
├─ main branch (unchanged)
└─ feat-link-color branch (merged)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FORK vs COLLABORATOR - FINAL COMPARISON
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

COLLABORATOR:
   ├─ Owner adds you
   ├─ You get push access to original repo
   ├─ Push to original branches
   ├─ Usually still use PR for main
   ├─ Good for team members
   ├─ Less secure (owner must trust)
   └─ Faster for trusted people

FORK:
   ├─ No permission needed
   ├─ You own your copy
   ├─ Push to your fork
   ├─ Submit PR to original
   ├─ Good for open source
   ├─ More secure (no direct access)
   └─ Industry standard

Use COLLABORATOR when:
   ├─ Working in team
   ├─ Private project
   ├─ Owner trusts you
   └─ Internal development

Use FORK when:
   ├─ Contributing to open source
   ├─ Don't know owner
   ├─ Random internet person
   ├─ Professional standard
   └─ Security minded

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COMPLETE FORK WORKFLOW
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

YOU (Random person):
   1. Go to original repo
   2. Click "Fork"
   3. Creates: your-username/network-automation
   4. Clone your fork locally
   5. Create branch: feat-link-color
   6. Edit styles.css (link color change)
   7. Commit: "changed link color"
   8. Push to your fork
      $ git push origin feat-link-color
      └─ SUCCESS ✓ (you own fork)

ORIGINAL OWNER:
   1. Sees PR from your fork
   2. Reviews code
   3. Likes the change
   4. Approves PR
   5. Merges PR
   6. Your code in their repo ✓

RESULT:
   ├─ You contributed to open source ✓
   ├─ Code in original repo ✓
   ├─ You don't own original repo
   ├─ But your code is there ✓
   └─ Collaboration successful! 🎉

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━���━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
GITHUB COLLABORATION SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Two main ways to collaborate:

1. COLLABORATOR APPROACH:
   └─ Owner adds you
   └─ You push to original repo
   └─ PR for main (usually)
   └─ Good for teams

2. FORK APPROACH:
   └─ You fork repo
   └─ You push to your fork
   └─ PR to original repo
   └─ Good for open source

Both use:
   ├─ Branches (feature branches)
   ├─ Commits (with good messages)
   ├─ Push (to your repo)
   ├─ Pull Requests (for review)
   ├─ Code review (quality)
   ├─ Merging (final step)
   └─ Pull locally (sync)

GitHub is about:
   ├─ Storing code remotely
   ├─ Collaborating on code
   ├─ Code review
   ├─ Version control
   ├─ Team communication
   ├─ Project management
   └─ Community contribution

Key features:
   ├─ Forks (copy repo)
   ├─ Pull Requests (propose changes)
   ├─ Collaborators (team members)
   ├─ Branch protection (safety)
   ├─ Code review (quality)
   ├─ Issues (discussion)
   └─ Discussions (community)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
QUICK WORKFLOW COMPARISON
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

COLLABORATOR WORKFLOW:
   1. Create branch
   2. Make changes
   3. Commit & push (to original repo)
   4. Create PR
   5. Owner reviews
   6. Owner merges
   7. Pull locally

FORK WORKFLOW:
   1. Fork repo (copy to your account)
   2. Clone your fork
   3. Create branch
   4. Make changes
   5. Commit & push (to your fork)
   6. Create PR (to original repo)
   7. Owner reviews
   8. Owner merges
   9. Pull locally

Key difference:
   └─ Fork has 1 extra step (fork + clone your fork)
   └─ But doesn't need permission
   └─ More secure & professional

GIT & GITHUB - COMPLETE LEARNING SUMMARY

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT YOU'VE LEARNED
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

This course covered essential Git & GitHub knowledge:

✓ Git fundamentals
✓ GitHub basics
✓ Collaboration workflow
✓ Professional practices

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
GIT PART
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Key concepts learned:

1. COMMITS
   What: Snapshots of your code
   Why: Track changes
   How: $ git commit -m "message"

   Know:
   ├─ What commits are
   ├─ How to create commits
   ├─ Good commit messages
   ├─ Commit history
   └─ Why commits matter

2. BRANCHES
   What: Parallel versions of code
   Why: Work on features separately
   How: $ git checkout -b branch-name

   Know:
   ├─ Creating branches
   ├─ Switching branches
   ├─ Working on branches
   ├─ Merging branches
   └─ Branch naming conventions

3. GIT COMMANDS
   Essential commands:
   ├─ $ git init (initialize repo)
   ├─ $ git add (stage changes)
   ├─ $ git commit (create snapshot)
   ├─ $ git branch (manage branches)
   ├─ $ git checkout (switch branches)
   ├─ $ git merge (combine branches)
   ├─ $ git pull (get updates)
   ├─ $ git push (upload code)
   └─ $ git status (check status)

4. LOCAL WORKFLOW
   Basic workflow:
   1. Make changes
   2. Stage with $ git add
   3. Commit with $ git commit
   4. Push with $ git push
   5. Pull updates with $ git pull

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
GITHUB PART
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Key concepts learned:

1. REMOTE REPOSITORY
   What: Code stored on GitHub servers
   Why: Backup & collaboration
   How: Push & pull code

   Know:
   ├─ What remote repos are
   ├─ How to connect (origin)
   ├─ Pushing code up
   ├─ Pulling code down
   └─ Keeping in sync

2. FORKS
   What: Copy of someone's repo
   Why: Contribute to open source
   How: Click "Fork" button

   Know:
   ├─ Creating forks
   ├─ Owning your fork
   ├─ Independent copy
   ├─ Submitting PRs from fork
   └─ Open source contribution

3. COLLABORATORS
   What: Team members with push access
   Why: Working together on project
   How: Owner adds collaborators

   Know:
   ├─ Adding team members
   ├─ Push permissions
   ├─ Branch protection
   ├─ Private repos
   └─ Team workflow

4. PULL REQUESTS (PR)
   What: Formal code review mechanism
   Why: Quality control & discussion
   How: Create PR on GitHub

   Know:
   ├─ Creating PRs
   ├─ Code review process
   ├─ Approval process
   ├─ Merging code
   ├─ Handling feedback
   ├─ Updating PRs
   └─ Professional standard

5. BRANCH PROTECTION
   What: Rules on branches (usually main)
   Why: Prevent direct pushes
   How: Owner sets up rules

   Know:
   ├─ Protected branches
   ├─ Require PR before merge
   ├─ Require code review
   ├─ Require status checks
   └─ Production safety

6. ISSUES
   What: Discussion & tracking
   Why: Bug reports, feature requests
   How: Create issue on GitHub

   Know:
   ├─ Creating issues
   ├─ Linking PRs to issues
   ├─ Issue discussion
   ├─ Auto-closing issues
   └─ Project management

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COLLABORATION WORKFLOWS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

You learned two main collaboration approaches:

APPROACH 1: COLLABORATOR
   Process:
   1. Owner adds you as collaborator
   2. You get push access
   3. Create feature branch
   4. Make changes
   5. Commit & push to original repo
   6. Create PR
   7. Owner reviews & merges

   Best for:
   ├─ Team projects
   ├─ Private repos
   ├─ Trusted members
   └─ Internal development

APPROACH 2: FORK
   Process:
   1. Fork repo (copy to your account)
   2. Clone your fork
   3. Create feature branch
   4. Make changes
   5. Commit & push to YOUR fork
   6. Create PR to original repo
   7. Owner reviews & merges

   Best for:
   ├─ Open source projects
   ├─ Random contributors
   ├─ No permission needed
   └─ Industry standard

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PROFESSIONAL WORKFLOW (COMPLETE)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Standard professional workflow:

Step 1: Create feature branch
   $ git checkout -b feat-new-feature

Step 2: Make meaningful changes
   Edit files
   Test your code
   Ensure quality

Step 3: Commit regularly
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "descriptive message"
   (Commit frequently for history)

Step 4: Push to remote
   $ git push origin feat-new-feature
   (Your branch on GitHub)

Step 5: Create Pull Request
   Go to GitHub
   Click "New pull request"
   Add title & description
   Link related issues

Step 6: Code Review
   Team reviews code
   Leave comments
   Request changes if needed

Step 7: Respond to feedback
   Make requested changes
   Commit changes
   Push again
   PR updates automatically

Step 8: Approval & Merge
   After approval
   Owner merges PR
   Code in main ✓

Step 9: Delete branch
   Delete feature branch
   Keep repo clean

Step 10: Sync locally
   $ git pull origin main
   Get latest code

Step 11: Continue cycle
   Create new branch
   New feature
   Repeat process

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
KEY PRINCIPLES YOU LEARNED
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

1. VERSION CONTROL
   └─ Track all code changes
   └─ Know who changed what & when
   └─ Revert if needed

2. COLLABORATION
   └─ Multiple people working together
   └─ No conflicting changes
   └─ Team coordination

3. CODE QUALITY
   └─ Code review before merging
   └─ Catch bugs early
   └─ Maintain standards

4. SAFETY
   └─ Protected main branch
   └─ No accidental production breaks
   └─ Professional practices

5. COMMUNICATION
   └─ Commit messages explain changes
   └─ PR descriptions provide context
   └─ Comments for discussion

6. BACKUP
   └─ Code on remote server
   └─ Multiple copies
   └─ Never lose work

7. TRANSPARENCY
   └─ All changes visible
   └─ Full history tracked
   └─ Accountability

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COMMON REAL-WORLD SCENARIOS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Scenario 1: NEW DEVELOPER IN TEAM
   1. Clone repo
   2. Create feature branch
   3. Make changes
   4. Push to GitHub
   5. Create PR
   6. Team reviews
   7. Merge
   ✓ Contributing!

Scenario 2: OPEN SOURCE CONTRIBUTOR
   1. Fork repo
   2. Clone your fork
   3. Create feature branch
   4. Make improvements
   5. Push to your fork
   6. Create PR to original
   7. Maintainer reviews
   8. Merge
   ✓ Contributing to open source!

Scenario 3: BUG FIX
   1. Create issue (bug report)
   2. Create branch (bugfix/issue-123)
   3. Fix the bug
   4. Commit fix
   5. Push branch
   6. Create PR
   7. Team verifies fix works
   8. Merge
   9. Issue closed
   ✓ Bug fixed!

Scenario 4: TEAM REVIEW
   1. Create PR
   2. Team members review
   3. Leave comments
   4. Request changes
   5. You update code
   6. Team approves
   7. Merge
   ✓ Quality assured!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
ESSENTIAL GIT COMMANDS SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Setup:
   $ git config --global user.name "Your Name"
   $ git config --global user.email "email@example.com"

Initialize:
   $ git init
   $ git clone URL

Branches:
   $ git branch (list branches)
   $ git checkout -b branch-name (create & switch)
   $ git checkout branch-name (switch)
   $ git branch -d branch-name (delete)

Working:
   $ git status (check status)
   $ git diff (see changes)
   $ git add . (stage all)
   $ git commit -m "message" (commit)

Remote:
   $ git push origin branch-name (upload)
   $ git pull origin branch-name (download)
   $ git fetch (get updates without merge)

Merging:
   $ git merge branch-name (merge locally)

History:
   $ git log (see commits)
   $ git log --oneline (compact view)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Advanced GitHub:
   ├─ GitHub Actions (CI/CD)
   ├─ GitHub Pages (hosting)
   ├─ GitHub Packages (package management)

   ├─ Code scanning

   └─ Make mistakes & learn
