34.GITHUB ACTIONS - SIMPLE INTRODUCTION

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT IS GITHUB ACTIONS?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

GitHub Actions = Automation service which,
Automates tasks automatically without you doing it

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WITHOUT GITHUB ACTIONS vs WITH GITHUB ACTIONS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

WITHOUT:
   Push code → You test → You deploy → You monitor
   Time: Hours of manual work ✗

WITH:
   Push code → Automatic test → Auto deploy → Auto monitor
   Time: 5 minutes, no work from you ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
BENEFITS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

✓ Saves time (no manual work)
✓ No errors (consistent process)
✓ Fast feedback (minutes)
✓ Professional standard
✓ Free to use

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
THREE KEY CONCEPTS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

1. WORKFLOW = Complete automation
2. JOB = Group of tasks
3. STEP = Single task

Workflow
   ├─ Job 1
   │  ├─ Step 1
   │  ├─ Step 2
   │  └─ Step 3
   └─ Job 2
      ├─ Step 1
      └─ Step 2

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PREREQUISITES
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

✓ GitHub account
✓ Basic Git knowledge
✓ Basic GitHub knowledge
======================================================
35.GITHUB ACTIONS - BUILDING BLOCKS (Network-Automation Project)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
THREE MAIN BUILDING BLOCKS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

1. WORKFLOWS
2. JOBS
3. STEPS

Relationship:

Repository (on GitHub)
   ↓
Workflow (automation process)
   ├─ Job 1 (group of tasks)
   │  ├─ Step 1
   │  ├─ Step 2
   │  └─ Step 3
   │
   ├─ Job 2 (group of tasks)
   │  ├─ Step 1
   │  └─ Step 2
   │
   └─ Job 3 (group of tasks)
      └─ Step 1

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
1. WORKFLOWS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

What is a Workflow?
   Automation process attached to GitHub repository
   File: .github/workflows/name.yml
   Contains: One or more jobs

For Network-Automation Project:

File location:
   Network-Automation/
   └─ .github/
      └─ workflows/
         ├─ test.yml
         ├─ build.yml
         └─ deploy.yml

Example: test.yml

name: "Test Network Automation"

on:
   push:
      branches: [main]

Purpose:
   When code pushed to main
   Run tests on VPN automation code
   Check all Python files work

Triggers/Events:
   push → Workflow runs when code pushed
   pull_request → Workflow runs on PR
   schedule → Runs on time schedule
   workflow_dispatch → Manual trigger

Key points:
   ✓ Multiple workflows per repo
   ✓ Each workflow = one automation process
   ✓ Contains jobs
   ✓ Triggered by events

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
2. JOBS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

What is a Job?
   Set of steps that run together
   Belongs to: Workflow
   Contains: One or more steps

For Network-Automation Project:

Example workflow with jobs:

name: "Build and Test"

on: push

jobs:
   test-python:
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - run: python -m pytest tests/

   build-docker:
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - run: docker build -t network-automation .

   deploy-vpn:
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - run: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py

Jobs explained:

JOB 1: test-python
   What: Test all Python code
   Runs on: ubuntu-latest
   Purpose: Verify code works
   Tests: Unit tests for VPN automation

JOB 2: build-docker
   What: Build Docker container
   Runs on: ubuntu-latest
   Purpose: Create container image
   Container: Includes vpn_automation code

JOB 3: deploy-vpn
   What: Deploy automation script
   Runs on: ubuntu-latest
   Purpose: Run build.py for deployment
   Script: backend/vpn_automation/build.py

Execution:
   Default: Jobs run in PARALLEL
   Result: All 3 jobs run at same time
   Faster: Finishes sooner

Can make sequential:

jobs:
   test-python:
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - run: python -m pytest

   build-docker:
      needs: test-python
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - run: docker build -t network-automation .

   deploy-vpn:
      needs: build-docker
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - run: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py

With "needs":
   test-python runs first
   → if passes, build-docker runs
   → if passes, deploy-vpn runs
   → if any fails, stops there

Key points:
   ✓ Group related steps
   ✓ Specify runner (ubuntu-latest)
   ✓ Can run parallel (default)
   ✓ Can be sequential (needs)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
3. STEPS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

What is a Step?
   Individual task or command
   Belongs to: Job
   Contains: Script or action

Two types of steps:

TYPE 1: Shell Script (run:)
   Execute Python/command
   Example:
      - run: python -m pytest
      - run: python backend/vpn_automation/main.py

TYPE 2: Action (uses:)
   Pre-built tool
   Example:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3

For Network-Automation Project:

Example steps for VPN automation:

steps:
   - name: "Checkout code"
     uses: actions/checkout@v3

   - name: "Set up Python"
     uses: actions/setup-python@v4
     with:
       python-version: '3.9'

   - name: "Install dependencies"
     run: pip install -r requirements.txt

   - name: "Run VPN discovery"
     run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py

   - name: "Run VPN client"
     run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py

   - name: "Execute build"
     run: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py

Steps breakdown:

STEP 1: Checkout
   Action: actions/checkout@v3
   What: Gets your code from GitHub
   Why: Need code to run

STEP 2: Setup Python
   Action: actions/setup-python@v4
   What: Installs Python 3.9
   Why: Code is Python, need Python environment

STEP 3: Install dependencies
   Command: pip install -r requirements.txt
   What: Installs all Python packages
   Why: Your VPN automation needs libraries

STEP 4: Run discovery
   Command: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py
   What: Runs network discovery script
   Why: Part of automation

STEP 5: Run client
   Command: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py
   What: Runs VPN client script
   Why: Test VPN client functionality

STEP 6: Execute build
   Command: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py
   What: Runs build script
   Why: Deploy/build application

Execution:
   Steps run: ONE AFTER ANOTHER (sequential)
   Order: Matters (can't run client before discovery)
   If one fails: Rest don't run

Key points:
   ✓ Define actual work
   ✓ Can be commands or actions
   ✓ Execute in order
   ✓ Minimum 1 per job

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COMPLETE NETWORK-AUTOMATION EXAMPLE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

File: Network-Automation/.github/workflows/vpn-automation.yml

name: "VPN Automation Pipeline"

on:
   push:
      branches: [main]

jobs:
   test-vpn:
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - name: "Checkout code"
           uses: actions/checkout@v3

         - name: "Setup Python 3.9"
           uses: actions/setup-python@v4
           with:
             python-version: '3.9'

         - name: "Install dependencies"
           run: pip install -r requirements.txt

         - name: "Run tests"
           run: python -m pytest tests/

   build-automation:
      needs: test-vpn
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - name: "Checkout code"
           uses: actions/checkout@v3

         - name: "Setup Python 3.9"
           uses: actions/setup-python@v4
           with:
             python-version: '3.9'

         - name: "Install dependencies"
           run: pip install -r requirements.txt

         - name: "Run VPN discovery"
           run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py

         - name: "Run VPN client"
           run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py

         - name: "Execute build"
           run: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
EXECUTION FLOW (Network-Automation)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

You push code:
$ git push origin main
   ↓
Workflow triggered: "VPN Automation Pipeline"
   ↓
JOB 1: test-vpn (starts)
   ├─ STEP 1: Checkout code
   ├─ STEP 2: Setup Python 3.9
   ├─ STEP 3: Install dependencies
   └─ STEP 4: Run tests
   ↓ (only if all steps pass)
JOB 2: build-automation (starts)
   ├─ STEP 1: Checkout code
   ├─ STEP 2: Setup Python 3.9
   ├─ STEP 3: Install dependencies
   ├─ STEP 4: Run VPN discovery
   ├─ STEP 5: Run VPN client
   └─ STEP 6: Execute build
   ↓
Done! VPN automation deployed ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
YOUR PROJECT FILES USED
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

From your Network-Automation project:

backend/vpn_automation/
├─ __init__.py (imports)
├─ asa_vpn_ops.py (VPN operations)
├─ build.py (build/deploy script)
├─ client.py (VPN client)
├─ config.py (configuration)
├─ discovery.py (network discovery)
└─ main.py (main entry point)

GitHub Actions will run:

1. Tests on all Python files
2. discovery.py - discover networks
3. client.py - test VPN client
4. build.py - build/deploy application

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
KEY TAKEAWAYS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

WORKFLOW:
   ✓ File: .github/workflows/vpn-automation.yml
   ✓ Triggered by: push to main
   ✓ Contains: Multiple jobs

JOB:
   ✓ test-vpn: Tests code
   ✓ build-automation: Builds/deploys
   ✓ Runs sequentially (needs)

STEP:
   ✓ Checkout, Setup Python, Install deps
   ✓ Run discovery, client, build scripts
   ✓ Execute in order

Result:
   Every push → Auto test → Auto deploy
   Your VPN automation always current! ✓


WITHOUT GITHUB ACTIONS vs WITH GITHUB ACTIONS

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WITHOUT GITHUB ACTIONS - MANUAL WORKFLOW
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ DEVELOPER: Local Development                            │
│                                                         │
│ You write code:                                         │
│ - Edit backend/vpn_automation/discovery.py             │
│ - Edit backend/vpn_automation/client.py                │
│ - Save files                                            │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ LOCAL TESTING (MANUAL)                                  │
│                                                         │
│ YOU RUN TESTS ON YOUR LAPTOP:                           │
│ $ python -m pytest tests/                               │
│ Wait... (5-10 minutes)                                  │
│ Check results manually                                  │
│ If tests fail:                                          │
│   - Fix code                                            │
│   - Run tests again                                     │
│   - Repeat until pass ✓                                 │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ GIT: Commit Changes (MANUAL)                            │
│                                                         │
│ $ git add .                                             │
│ $ git commit -m "improved VPN discovery"               │
│ (You do this manually)                                  │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌───────────────────��─────────────────────────────────────┐
│ GITHUB: Push to Repository (MANUAL)                     │
│                                                         │
│ $ git push origin main                                  │
│ Code uploaded to: Network-Automation repo               │
│ (You push manually)                                     │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ MERGE PR (MANUAL)                                       │
│                                                         │
│ You go to GitHub website                               │
│ Click "Merge pull request" button                      │
│ Confirm merge                                           │
│ (All manual - you click)                                │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ BUILD APPLICATION (MANUAL)                              │
│                                                         │
│ You SSH to server:                                      │
│ $ ssh user@server.com                                   │
│ $ cd /app/network-automation                            │
│ $ git pull origin main                                  │
│ Wait... (downloading code)                              │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ INSTALL DEPENDENCIES (MANUAL)                           │
│                                                         │
│ You run:                                                │
│ $ pip install -r requirements.txt                       │
│ Wait... (installing packages - 5-10 minutes)           │
│ Hope nothing breaks!                                    │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ RUN DISCOVERY (MANUAL)                                  │
│                                                         │
│ You run:                                                │
│ $ python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py       │
│ Wait... (network discovery - 5-15 minutes)             │
│ Check results manually                                  │
│ If error: Debug manually                                │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ RUN CLIENT (MANUAL)                                     │
│                                                         │
│ You run:                                                │
│ $ python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py          │
│ Wait... (client execution)                              │
│ Check if VPN connects                                   │
│ If fails: Debug manually                                │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ BUILD APPLICATION (MANUAL)                              │
│                                                         │
│ You run:                                                │
│ $ python backend/vpn_automation/build.py               │
│ Wait... (building - 10-20 minutes)                     │
│ Check build output                                      │
│ If error: Fix and rebuild                               │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ DEPLOYMENT COMPLETE (MAYBE)                             │
│                                                         │
│ Status: Done (if everything worked)                    │
│ Total time: 1-3 HOURS of YOUR manual work              │
│ Stress level: HIGH 😰                                  │
│ Error chance: HIGH ⚠️                                  │
│                                                         │
│ You now monitor:                                        │
│ - Check logs manually                                   │
│ - Test application manually                             │
│ - Hope nothing breaks                                   │
│ - Be available 24/7 for fixes                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

SUMMARY WITHOUT ACTIONS:
├─ Manual testing ✗
├─ Manual git operations ✗
├─ Manual SSH to server ✗
├─ Manual dependency install ✗
├─ Manual script execution ✗
├─ Manual build process ✗
├─ Manual monitoring ✗
├─ Time: 1-3 hours ✗
├─ Error prone ✗
└─ YOU do everything ✗

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WITH GITHUB ACTIONS - AUTOMATED WORKFLOW
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ DEVELOPER: Local Development                            │
│                                                         │
│ You write code:                                         │
│ - Edit backend/vpn_automation/discovery.py             │
│ - Edit backend/vpn_automation/client.py                │
│ - Test locally                                          │
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ GIT: Commit Changes                                     │
│                                                         │
│ $ git add .                                             │
│ $ git commit -m "improved VPN discovery"               │

37.Creating First Work FLow
GITHUB ACTIONS - FIRST WORKFLOW (Network-Automation Project)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WORKFLOW BUILDING BLOCKS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

WORKFLOWS:
   └─ Attached to network-automation repository
   └─ Contain one or more Jobs
   └─ Triggered upon Events

JOBS:
   └─ Define a Runner (execution environment)
   └─ Contain one or more Steps
   └─ Run in parallel (default) or sequential
   └─ Can be conditional

STEPS:
   └─ Execute shell script or Action
   └─ Run VPN automation scripts
   └─ Steps are executed in order
   └─ Can be conditional
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Your first workflow for network-automation:

1. Go to Actions tab in your repo
2. Set up workflow in browser editor
3. File path: .github/workflows/first-test.yml
4. Write YAML:
   ├─ name: VPN Automation Test
   ├─ on: workflow_dispatch
   ├─ jobs:
   │  └─ test-vpn:
   │     ├─ runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   │     └─ steps: (your VPN test steps)
5. Commit changes
6. Run workflow manually

Next: Replace echo commands with real VPN scripts! 🚀
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
CREATING FIRST WORKFLOW (Your Network-Automation Repo)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Repository: MANISH-GANVIR / network-automation (Public)

Step 1: Click "Actions" tab
   └─ Top menu → Actions

Step 2: Choose "Simple workflow" template
   └─ Or "Set up a workflow yourself"

Step 3: File path is auto-created
   └─ network-automation / .github / workflows / main.yml

Step 4: Write workflow in editor

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WORKFLOW FILE STRUCTURE (For VPN Automation)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

1. NAME - What is this workflow?

name: VPN Automation Test

2. ON - When should it run?

on: workflow_dispatch
   └─ Manual trigger (user clicks button)

3. JOBS - What work to do?

jobs:
   test-vpn:
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         (steps go here)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SIMPLE WORKFLOW CODE (First Version)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

File: .github/workflows/first-test.yml

name: VPN Automation Test

on: workflow_dispatch

jobs:
   test-vpn:
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - name: Checkout VPN code
           run: echo "Checking out network-automation code"

         - name: Setup Python
           run: echo "Setting up Python environment"

         - name: Run VPN discovery
           run: echo "Running VPN discovery script"

         - name: Verify VPN setup
           run: echo "VPN automation test complete!"

What this does:
   ├─ Checks out your network-automation code
   ├─ Sets up Python
   ├─ Runs discovery script
   ├─ Verifies setup
   └─ All manual trigger (workflow_dispatch)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
YAML SYNTAX EXPLAINED
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Line 1: name: VPN Automation Test
   ├─ Reserved keyword: name
   ├─ Colon: :
   └─ Value: VPN Automation Test (your workflow name)

Line 2: on: workflow_dispatch
   ├─ Reserved keyword: on
   ├─ Colon: :
   └─ Value: workflow_dispatch (manual trigger)

Line 3: jobs:
   ├─ Reserved keyword: jobs
   ├─ Colon:
   └─ No value → has nested fields below

Line 4: test-vpn: (indented with tab)
   ├─ Job identifier (you choose name)
   ├─ Colon:
   └─ No value → has nested fields below

Line 5: runs-on: ubuntu-latest (indented 2x)
   ├─ Reserved keyword: runs-on
   ├─ Colon:
   └─ Value: ubuntu-latest (Linux runner)

Line 6: steps: (indented 2x)
   ├─ Reserved keyword: steps
   ├─ Colon:
   └─ No value → has list below

Lines 7-14: Step list (indented with dash)
   ├─ Dash: - (indicates list item)
   ├─ name: (step identifier)
   ├─ run: (command to execute)
   └─ Multiple steps = multiple dashes

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
RESERVED KEYWORDS (Must be exact)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

✓ name (workflow name)
✓ on (trigger)
✓ jobs (container for jobs)
✓ runs-on (runner/environment)
✓ steps (container for steps)
✓ run (shell command)

✗ NOT reserved (you choose):
   ├─ Workflow name value (VPN Automation Test)
   ├─ Job identifier (test-vpn)
   ├─ Step names (Checkout VPN code, etc.)
   └─ Custom values

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
INDENTATION RULES (YAML Format)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━��━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

IMPORTANT: Indentation matters!

No indent:
   name: VPN Automation Test
   on: workflow_dispatch

Indent 1 level (tab):
   jobs:
      test-vpn:

Indent 2 levels (2 tabs):
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:

Indent with dash for lists:
      steps:
         - name: Checkout VPN code
         - name: Setup Python
         - name: Run VPN discovery

Wrong indentation = Error! ✗

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FILE LOCATION (Must be exact)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Your network-automation repo:
   network-automation/
   └─ .github/ (hidden folder, must start with dot)
      └─ workflows/ (subfolder, exact name)
         └─ first-test.yml (your workflow file)

GitHub will NOT detect workflow if path is wrong!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 1: WORKFLOW NAME
━━━��━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

First, give workflow a name

name: VPN Automation Test

What it does:
   └─ Identifies this workflow
   └─ Shows in Actions tab
   └─ Describes what it does

Your workflow names:
   ├─ VPN Automation Test
   ├─ Discovery Test
   ├─ Client Connection Test
   └─ Build and Deploy VPN

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 2: TRIGGER (ON)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Second, define when workflow runs

on: workflow_dispatch

What it does:
   └─ workflow_dispatch = manual trigger
   └─ You click button to run
   └─ Not automatic

Other triggers for your VPN project:
   ├─ push (when you push VPN code)
   ├─ pull_request (when PR to test code)
   └─ schedule (run tests daily)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 3: JOB DEFINITION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Third, define jobs (work to do)

jobs:
   test-vpn:

What it does:
   └─ "jobs" is reserved keyword
   └─ "test-vpn" is job identifier (you choose)
   └─ Job will test VPN automation

Your job names:
   ├─ test-vpn (test VPN code)
   ├─ run-discovery (run discovery script)
   ├─ test-client (test VPN client)
   └─ build-automation (build automation package)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 4: RUNNER ENVIRONMENT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Fourth, specify where job runs

runs-on: ubuntu-latest

What it does:
   └─ "runs-on" is reserved keyword
   └─ ubuntu-latest = Linux environment
   └─ Your VPN code runs on this Linux machine

Available runners:
   ├─ ubuntu-latest (Linux - recommended for Python)
   ├─ windows-latest (Windows)
   └─ macos-latest (macOS)

Your VPN project:
   └─ Use: ubuntu-latest
   └─ Reason: Python code works best on Linux

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 5: WORKFLOW STEPS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Fifth, define actual work (steps for VPN automation)

steps:
   - name: Checkout VPN code
     run: echo "Checking out network-automation code"

   - name: Setup Python
     run: echo "Setting up Python environment"

   - name: Run VPN discovery
     run: echo "Running VPN discovery script"

   - name: Verify VPN setup
     run: echo "VPN automation test complete!"

What each step does:

STEP 1: Checkout VPN code
   └─ Gets your network-automation code from GitHub
   └─ Makes it available in the runner

STEP 2: Setup Python
   └─ Installs Python environment
   └─ Needed for your Python scripts

STEP 3: Run VPN discovery
   └─ Runs: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py
   └─ Tests network discovery functionality

STEP 4: Verify VPN setup
   └─ Confirms everything worked
   └─ Provides feedback

Steps execute:
   ├─ One after another (sequential)
   ├─ In order (1, 2, 3, 4)
   ├─ If one fails, rest skip
   └─ All must complete for success

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
HOW TO CREATE IN YOUR REPO
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

In GitHub:

Step 1: Go to network-automation repo
   └─ github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation

Step 2: Click "Actions" tab
   └─ Top menu

Step 3: Click "Set up a workflow yourself"
   └─ Or choose template

Step 4: Paste workflow code above
   └─ In the text editor

Step 5: Change filename (optional)
   └─ main.yml → first-test.yml

Step 6: Click "Start commit"
   └─ Green button

Step 7: Add commit message
   └─ "Add first VPN automation workflow"

Step 8: Click "Commit changes"
   └─ File created at: .github/workflows/first-test.yml

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
RUN YOUR FIRST WORKFLOW
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

After committing:

Step 1: Go to "Actions" tab
   └─ See your workflow listed

Step 2: Click on workflow
   └─ "VPN Automation Test"

Step 3: Click "Run workflow"
   └─ Dropdown → Run workflow button

Step 4: Watch it run
   └─ See job: test-vpn
   └─ See each step execute
   └─ Watch output in real-time

Step 5: See results
   ├─ Green checkmark = Success ✓
   ├─ Red X = Failed ✗
   └─ View logs for details

===================================================
38. Running the First Workflow
-------------------------------
GITHUB ACTIONS - SIMPLE COMPLETE GUIDE

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WORKFLOW FILES = YOUR CODE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Stored in Git repository:
   network-automation/
   └─ .github/workflows/vpn-automation.yml

When you create/edit:
   ├─ Creates commit in Git
   ├─ Part of your project code
   ├─ Version controlled
   └─ Can revert anytime

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 1: CREATE WORKFLOW FILE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Go to: github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation
Click: Actions tab
Choose: "Set up a workflow yourself"

Write workflow code:

name: VPN Automation Pipeline

on:
   push:
      branches: [main]

jobs:
   test-vpn:
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - uses: actions/checkout@v3
         - run: echo "Testing VPN automation"

   build-automation:
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      steps:
         - uses: actions/checkout@v3
         - run: echo "Building VPN automation"

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 2: COMMIT WORKFLOW
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

After writing code:

1. Click "Start commit" button
2. Add message: "Add VPN automation workflow"
3. Choose branch: main
4. Click "Commit changes"

Result:
   ├─ File saved: .github/workflows/vpn-automation.yml
   ├─ Workflow ready
   └─ GitHub detects it ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 3: WORKFLOW FILES ARE PART OF CODE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Your repository structure:

network-automation/
├─ backend/
│  └─ vpn_automation/
│     ├─ discovery.py (your code)
│     ├─ client.py (your code)
│     └─ build.py (your code)
│
├─ .github/
│  └─ workflows/
│     └─ vpn-automation.yml (workflow = your code too!)
│
└─ README.md

Both discovery.py + workflow.yml:
   ├─ In GitHub repo
   ├─ Version controlled
   ├─ Part of project
   └─ Tracked in Git

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 4: WHAT TRIGGERS WORKFLOW? (on: push)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Your workflow has:

on:
   push:
      branches: [main]

This means:
   ├─ Trigger event: push
   ├─ Trigger branch: main
   └─ When activated: After push to main

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 5: EDIT & PUSH CODE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Now in your local machine:

Edit your code:
   $ code backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py
   (Fix bug or add feature)

Commit locally:
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "fix discovery bug"

Push to GitHub:
   $ git push origin main

After push:
   └─ Code uploaded to GitHub ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 6: WORKFLOW AUTOMATICALLY TRIGGERS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

The moment you push:

GitHub automatically:

   ├─ Detects: "push to main branch"
   ├─ Looks in: .github/workflows/ folder
   ├─ Finds: vpn-automation.yml file
   ├─ Reads: on: push, branches: [main]
   ├─ Matches: Trigger condition ✓
   └─ STARTS WORKFLOW! 🚀

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 7: WORKFLOW RUNS (Automatic)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

GitHub Actions servers start:

Job 1: test-vpn
   ├─ Runner allocated: ubuntu-latest
   ├─ Step 1: Checkout code (gets discovery.py)
   ├─ Step 2: Run: echo "Testing VPN automation"
   └─ Result: ✓ Success

Job 2: build-automation
   ├─ Step 1: Checkout code
   ├─ Step 2: Run: echo "Building VPN automation"
   └─ Result: ✓ Success

Total time: 1-2 minutes (automatic)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 8: VIEW RESULTS (Actions Tab)
━━━��━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Go to: Actions tab

You see:
   ├─ Workflow name: "VPN Automation Pipeline"
   ├─ Status: Running 🟡 (or Success ✓)
   ├─ Job 1: test-vpn ✓
   ├─ Job 2: build-automation ✓
   └─ Time taken

Click on workflow run:
   ├─ See each job
   ├─ See each step
   ├─ Click step arrow to expand
   └─ See full output & logs

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COMPLETE TIMELINE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

10:00 - You edit discovery.py on laptop
10:05 - $ git commit -m "fix bug"
10:06 - $ git push origin main → Push complete ✓

10:06 - GitHub detects push → Workflow trigger! 🚀
10:07 - GitHub allocates runner
10:08 - Job 1: test-vpn runs
10:10 - Job 1 complete ✓
10:10 - Job 2: build-automation runs
10:12 - Job 2 complete ✓

10:12 - Workflow finished ✓
        Green checkmark in Actions tab
        Notification sent

YOU: Only pushed code + drank coffee! ☕

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 9: RE-RUN WORKFLOW (Optional)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Option 1: From Actions tab
   └─ Click "Run workflow" button
   └─ Select branch: main
   └─ Click "Run workflow"

Option 2: From results page
   └─ Click "Re-run all jobs" button

You can run unlimited times ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SUMMARY - COMPLETE FLOW
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

1. Create workflow file (.github/workflows/name.yml)
2. Commit workflow to repo
3. Edit your code (discovery.py)
4. Commit code locally
5. Push code to GitHub
6. GitHub detects push
7. GitHub finds workflow file
8. Trigger matches (on: push)
9. Workflow automatically starts 🚀
10. Jobs run sequentially
11. Steps execute one by one
12. Results appear in Actions tab
13. You get notification
14. Done! ✓

YOU DO: Steps 3-5 only
GITHUB DOES: Steps 6-14 (automatic)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
KEY POINTS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━��━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

✓ Workflow = Your code (version controlled)
✓ Location: .github/workflows/
✓ File format: YAML (.yml)
✓ Trigger: on: push (or other events)
✓ Automatic: After push, workflow starts
✓ Servers: GitHub's servers run it
✓ View: Actions tab
✓ Free: No cost for public repos
✓ History: All runs logged
✓ Unlimited: Run as many times as needed

Done! Now you understand workflows! 🚀
==================================================
39.Running Multi-Line Shell Commands
-------------------------------------
MULTI-LINE COMMANDS - COMPLETE GUIDE (Network-Automation Project)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT IS THIS ABOUT?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Running MULTIPLE commands in a SINGLE workflow step
using the pipe symbol (|) so they share the same environment.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
YOUR VPN PROJECT WORKFLOW SEQUENCE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Your network-automation project needs:

Step 1: Install dependencies
   └─ pip install -r requirements.txt
   └─ Creates Python environment with packages

Step 2: Run discovery script
   └─ python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py
   └─ Discovers networks, creates network_data.json

Step 3: Run client script
   └─ python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py
   └─ Uses network_data.json from Step 2
   └─ Connects to VPN

Step 4: Build application
   └─ python backend/vpn_automation/build.py
   └─ Packages everything for deployment

KEY POINT: Each step DEPENDS on the previous step!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PROBLEM: WITHOUT MULTI-LINE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

If you write as 4 separate steps:

steps:
  - name: Install dependencies
    run: pip install -r requirements.txt

  - name: Run discovery
    run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py

  - name: Run client
    run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py

  - name: Build application
    run: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py

WHAT HAPPENS:

Step 1: Install dependencies
   ├─ GitHub creates Environment A
   ├─ Installs packages here
   ├─ network_data.json location: /tmp/
   └─ Step 1 ENDS → Environment A DESTROYED

Step 2: Run discovery
   ├─ GitHub creates FRESH Environment B
   ├─ Packages from Step 1 are GONE
   ├─ Script needs packages → ERROR!
   └─ FAILURE: Packages not found

Even if Step 2 somehow passes:

Step 3: Run client
   ├─ GitHub creates FRESH Environment C
   ├─ Looks for network_data.json
   ├─ File was created in Environment A
   ├─ Environment A is destroyed
   └─ FAILURE: File not found

PROBLEMS:

✗ Fresh environment per step
✗ Packages not available
✗ Files/data lost between steps
✗ Scripts fail
✗ Messy code (4 blocks)
✗ Slow execution (per-step overhead)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SOLUTION: WITH MULTI-LINE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Use pipe symbol (|) to combine all commands:

steps:
  - name: Setup and run VPN automation
    run: |
      pip install -r requirements.txt
      python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py
      python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py
      python backend/vpn_automation/build.py

WHAT HAPPENS:

Single Step: Setup and run VPN automation

   ├─ GitHub creates Environment A (ONCE)
   │
   ├─ Command 1: pip install -r requirements.txt
   │  ├─ Packages installed in Environment A
   │  ├─ Available for all following commands
   │  └─ Continues to Command 2
   │
   ├─ Command 2: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py
   │  ├─ Still in Environment A
   │  ├─ Packages available ✓
   │  ├─ Creates network_data.json in /tmp/
   │  ├─ File saved in Environment A
   │  └─ Continues to Command 3
   │
   ├─ Command 3: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py
   │  ├─ Still in Environment A
   │  ├─ network_data.json available ✓ (same env)
   │  ├─ Uses the file from Command 2
   │  ├─ Connects to VPN successfully
   │  └─ Continues to Command 4
   │
   ├─ Command 4: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py
   │  ├─ Still in Environment A
   │  ├─ All outputs available ✓
   │  ├─ Builds application successfully
   │  └─ Step ENDS → Environment A destroyed
   │
   └─ Result: SUCCESS ✓

BENEFITS:

✓ Same environment throughout
✓ Packages available for all commands
✓ Files/data preserved between commands
✓ Commands can share data
✓ Clean, organized code (1 block)
✓ Fast execution (no per-step overhead)
✓ Professional looking

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
REAL SEQUENCE IN YOUR PROJECT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

File dependencies in your project:

1. requirements.txt
   └─ Contains: requests, paramiko, netaddr, etc.

2. discovery.py
   ├─ Needs: packages from requirements.txt
   ├─ Creates: network_data.json
   └─ Scans network for devices

3. client.py
   ├─ Needs: packages from requirements.txt
   ├─ Needs: network_data.json (from discovery.py)
   ├─ Uses data to connect to VPN
   └─ Creates: connection_log.txt

4. build.py
   ├─ Needs: packages from requirements.txt
   ├─ Needs: network_data.json (from discovery.py)
   ├─ Needs: connection_log.txt (from client.py)
   ├─ Packages everything
   └─ Creates: deployment package

COMMAND CHAIN:

requirements.txt → discovery.py → client.py → build.py
       ↓              ↓              ↓            ↓
     Packages    Creates file    Uses file    Uses both
                                              files

Multi-line keeps all in SAME environment!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COMPLETE WORKFLOW EXAMPLE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

name: VPN Automation Pipeline

on: push

jobs:
  test-and-build:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3

      - name: Setup Python
        uses: actions/setup-python@v4
        with:
          python-version: '3.9'

      - name: Setup and run VPN automation
        run: |
          echo "Step 1: Installing dependencies..."
          pip install -r requirements.txt

          echo "Step 2: Running discovery..."
          python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py

          echo "Step 3: Running VPN client..."
          python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py

          echo "Step 4: Building application..."
          python backend/vpn_automation/build.py

          echo "All steps completed successfully!"

EXECUTION FLOW:

Workflow triggered → Job starts → Step 1: Setup Python (using action)
   ↓
Step 2: Setup and run VPN automation
   ├─ Environment A created
   ├─ Install dependencies (packages available)
   ├─ Run discovery (creates network_data.json in env)
   ├─ Run client (finds network_data.json in same env)
   ├─ Run build (uses all previous outputs in same env)
   ├─ All echo statements show progress
   └─ Environment A destroyed when step ends
   ↓
Workflow complete ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SYNTAX & RULES
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Correct syntax:

run: |
    command 1
    command 2
    command 3

Rules:

✓ Pipe symbol (|) after run:
✓ Each command on new line
✓ All commands indented (consistent spaces)
✓ Related commands grouped together
✓ All execute in ONE step
✓ Execute sequentially (one after another)
✓ If one fails, rest don't run

Wrong syntax:

run: |
command 1        ← No indentation (ERROR)
command 2

run:
  command 1      ← No pipe symbol (ERROR)
  command 2

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHEN TO USE MULTI-LINE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Use multi-line (|) when:

✓ Commands are related
✓ Commands depend on each other
✓ Commands share files/data
✓ Commands need same environment
✓ Want cleaner, organized code
✓ Want logical grouping

Examples in your project:

✓ Install + Run discovery + Run client + Build
✓ Setup environment + Run tests
✓ Install packages + Run multiple scripts
✓ Setup + Build + Deploy

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

What: Run multiple commands in one step

Why:
   ├─ Commands depend on each other
   ├─ Need same environment
   ├─ Need shared files/data
   └─ Cleaner code

How: Use pipe symbol (|)

Your project example:

Without multi-line:
   ├─ Step 1: Install (env A)
   ├─ Step 2: Discovery (fresh env B) ✗ Packages gone!
   ├─ Step 3: Client (fresh env C) ✗ File gone!
   └─ Step 4: Build (fresh env D) ✗ Everything gone!

With multi-line:
   └─ Single step: Install → Discovery → Client → Build
      (all in same Environment A) ✓ Everything works!

Result: Professional, working, clean workflows! 🚀

40. Onwards to a More Realistic Example
---------------------------------------
GITHUB ACTIONS WITH REACT PROJECT - COMPLETE GUIDE

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT IS THIS ABOUT?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Setting up GitHub Actions for a ReactJS project
to automatically run tests whenever code is pushed.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PROJECT STRUCTURE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

second-action-react-demo/
├─ src/
│  ├─ App.jsx (React component)
│  ├─ App.test.jsx (Test file)
│  ├─ index.js
│  └─ style.css
├─ node_modules/ (ignored in .gitignore)
├─ .gitignore (ignores node_modules)
├─ package.json (dependencies)
├─ package-lock.json
└─ README.md

Key files:
   ├─ package.json → defines npm commands
   ├─ App.test.jsx → contains automated tests
   ├─ .gitignore → ignores node_modules
   └─ .github/workflows/ → will add GitHub Actions here

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SETUP PROCESS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Step 1: INSTALL DEPENDENCIES LOCALLY

Command:
   $ npm install

What happens:
   ├─ Reads package.json
   ├─ Downloads all dependencies
   ├─ Creates node_modules/ folder
   ├─ Creates package-lock.json
   └─ Project ready to run

Step 2: RUN DEVELOPMENT SERVER

Command:
   $ npm run dev

What happens:
   ├─ Starts local development server
   ├─ Website accessible on localhost
   ├─ Can preview the React app
   └─ Hot reload on code changes

Step 3: RUN TESTS LOCALLY

Command:
   $ npm test

What happens:
   ├─ Finds all .test.jsx files
   ├─ Executes automated tests
   ├─ Shows results in console
   ├─ All tests should pass ✓
   └─ Example: Tests check if components render

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
ABOUT THE REACT PROJECT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

This is a simple ReactJS web application:

✓ Very basic demo app
✓ Not a complex real-world app
✓ Used for learning GitHub Actions
✓ You don't need React knowledge
✓ You don't need web dev knowledge
✓ Focus is on GitHub Actions workflow

What matters for GitHub Actions:

✓ Has npm commands (install, test, dev)
✓ Has automated tests
✓ Has dependencies
✓ Can be built and tested

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
.GITIGNORE FILE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Content:
   node_modules/
   dist/
   .env

What it does:

Ignores node_modules folder:
   ├─ Contains thousands of files
   ├─ Takes hundreds of MB
   ├─ Can be recreated with npm install
   ├─ Should NOT be version controlled
   ├─ Should NOT be pushed to GitHub
   └─ Only source code should be pushed

When code is cloned:
   ├─ node_modules/ is NOT downloaded
   ├─ .gitignore prevents it
   ├─ To get dependencies: npm install
   └─ Dependencies re-downloaded locally

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 1: INITIALIZE GIT REPOSITORY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

On your local system:

Command:
   $ git init

What happens:
   ├─ Creates .git folder (hidden)
   ├─ Initializes local Git repository
   ├─ Ready to track changes
   └─ Ready to connect to remote

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 2: CREATE INITIAL COMMIT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Commands:
   $ git add .
   $ git commit -m "Initial commit"

What happens:
   ├─ Stages all files
   ├─ .gitignore prevents node_modules from staging
   ├─ Creates initial commit
   ├─ Project history starts
   └─ Ready to push to remote

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 3: CREATE GITHUB REPOSITORY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

On GitHub.com:

1. Click "New Repository"
2. Name it: "second-action-react-demo"
3. Optional: Add description
4. Optional: Make it private
5. DO NOT add files (you have local code already)
6. Click "Create Repository"

Result:
   ├─ Empty remote repository created
   ├─ URL provided
   ├─ Ready to connect to local repo
   └─ No files yet

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━���━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 4: CONNECT LOCAL TO REMOTE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

On your local system:

Command:
   $ git remote add origin https://github.com/YOUR_USERNAME/second-action-react-demo.git

Replace:
   ├─ YOUR_USERNAME → your GitHub username
   ├─ second-action-react-demo → your repo name
   └─ https://... → your repo URL

What happens:
   ├─ Links local repo to remote
   ├─ "origin" = name of remote
   ├─ Connection configured
   └─ Ready to push

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 5: PUSH TO REMOTE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

First time setup:

Command:
   $ git push -u origin main

What happens:
   ├─ Pushes local main branch to remote
   ├─ -u flag = set upstream
   ├─ Links local main to remote main
   ├─ Future: just use "git push"
   ├─ You may be prompted for password
   ├─ Or use personal access token
   └─ Code uploaded to GitHub ✓

Future pushes:

Command:
   $ git push

What happens:
   ├─ Pushes to already-configured origin
   ├─ No need to specify branch
   ├─ Much simpler
   └─ Code uploaded to GitHub ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 6: VERIFY ON GITHUB
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Refresh your GitHub repository page:

You should see:
   ├─ src/ folder
   ├─ package.json
   ├─ package-lock.json
   ├─ README.md
   ├─ .gitignore
   └─ No node_modules/ (ignored)

Status:
   └─ Code uploaded successfully ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT HAPPENS LOCALLY vs GITHUB
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

YOUR LOCAL SYSTEM:

Files:
   ├─ src/ (source code)
   ├─ node_modules/ (1000s of files)
   ├─ .gitignore
   ├─ package.json
   └─ package-lock.json

What works:
   ├─ $ npm install (installs dependencies)
   ├─ $ npm run dev (runs development server)
   ├─ $ npm test (runs tests)
   └─ Everything available locally

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

GITHUB REPOSITORY:

Files:
   ├─ src/ (source code)
   ├─ .gitignore
   ├─ package.json
   ├─ package-lock.json
   └─ NO node_modules/ (ignored by .gitignore)

What you see:
   ├─ Only source code
   ├─ No dependencies
   ├─ Lightweight
   ├─ Easy to clone
   └─ "No actions related to it" yet

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHEN CODE IS CLONED (By collaborator):

Cloner gets:
   ├─ src/ (source code)
   ├─ .gitignore
   ├─ package.json
   ├─ package-lock.json
   └─ NO node_modules/

To run project:
   ├─ Must run: $ npm install
   ├─ Downloads all dependencies
   ├─ Creates node_modules/ locally
   ├─ Now: $ npm test works
   └─ Now: $ npm run dev works

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
NEXT: ADD GITHUB ACTIONS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Now that code is on GitHub:

Current state:
   └─ Repository has code
   └─ No workflows yet
   └─ No automated testing
   └─ No GitHub Actions

What we'll do next:

1. Create workflow file (.github/workflows/test.yml)
2. Add workflow to run npm test automatically
3. Trigger on: push (whenever code is pushed)
4. Results visible in Actions tab
5. Tests run automatically ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Setup Process:

1. Install dependencies locally
   └─ $ npm install

2. Initialize Git repository
   └─ $ git init

3. Create initial commit
   └─ $ git add . && git commit -m "Initial commit"

4. Create GitHub repository
   └─ On GitHub.com

5. Connect local to remote
   └─ $ git remote add origin <URL>

6. Push to GitHub
   └─ $ git push -u origin main

7. Verify on GitHub
   └─ Code uploaded, no node_modules

Next step:
   └─ Add GitHub Actions workflow
   └─ Automatically run tests
   └─ On every push

Your project is now on GitHub! 🚀
Ready to add GitHub Actions next!
===================================================
41. A New Workflow & The "push" Event
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WORKFLOW TRIGGERS/EVENTS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Repository events:
   ├─ push (code pushed)
   ├─ pull_request (PR opened/closed/etc)
   ├─ create (branch created)
   ├─ issues (issue opened/deleted)
   └─ Many more...

Other events:
   ├─ workflow_dispatch (manual)
   ├─ schedule (cron job)
   └─ workflow_call (reusable)

For React project → Use: on: push

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
CREATE WORKFLOW LOCALLY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Folder structure:

.github/workflows/test.yml

File content:

name: Test Project

on: push

jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Get code
        uses: actions/checkout@v3

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
KEY CONCEPT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Repository ≠ Workflow server

Repository:
   └─ Storage (your code)

Workflow server:
   └─ Execution (runs commands)

Problem:
   └─ Server doesn't have your code!

Solution:
   └─ First step: Download code
   └─ uses: actions/checkout@v3

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
ACTIONS vs RUN
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

run: (shell command)
   └─ run: npm install

uses: (pre-built action)
   └─ uses: actions/checkout@v3
   └─ Handles complex tasks
   └─ GitHub maintains

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

1. Create .github/workflows/test.yml
2. Define trigger: on: push
3. First step: download code (checkout)
4. Next steps: install deps, run tests
5. Push code → Workflow triggers
6. Results visible in Actions tab

Done! 🚀
=======================================================
42. Using Actions In Workflows:-
--------------------------------
ACTIONS - YOUR VPN PROJECT (ENGLISH)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT IS THE PROBLEM? (Without Actions)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Without Actions, you have to manually write complex commands:

Problem 1: DOWNLOAD CODE
   └─ You must write git clone command
   └─ Handle errors yourself
   └─ Manage directories yourself
   └─ Complex & error-prone

Problem 2: SETUP PYTHON
   └─ You must write apt-get install commands
   └─ Configure virtual environments
   └─ Setup pip yourself
   └─ Many lines of code
   └─ Easy to make mistakes

Problem 3: CODE REPETITION
   └─ Same complex commands in every workflow
   └─ Copy-paste code
   └─ Hard to maintain
   └─ If something changes, update everywhere

Problem 4: NO ERROR HANDLING
   └─ If step fails, next steps still run
   └─ No built-in safety
   └─ You handle everything
   └─ More code to write

Problem 5: NOT PROFESSIONAL
   └─ Lots of raw shell commands
   └─ Hard to read
   └─ Difficult to understand flow
   └─ Messy looking

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT IS THE SOLUTION? (With Actions)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Solution 1: SIMPLIFY CODE DOWNLOAD
   └─ Use: actions/checkout@v3
   └─ Just 2 lines
   └─ GitHub handles everything
   └─ Automatic error handling
   └─ Reliable

Solution 2: SIMPLIFY PYTHON SETUP
   └─ Use: actions/setup-python@v4
   └─ Just 3 lines
   └─ GitHub handles everything
   └─ Automatic error handling
   └─ Always works

Solution 3: REUSABILITY
   └─ Write once, use everywhere
   └─ GitHub maintains the action
   └─ Don't copy-paste code
   └─ Easy to maintain

Solution 4: BUILT-IN ERROR HANDLING
   └─ Actions handle errors automatically
   └─ Tested by GitHub
   └─ Professional quality
   └─ No need to handle edge cases

Solution 5: CLEAN & PROFESSIONAL
   └─ Less code
   └─ Easy to read
   └─ Clear intent
   └─ Professional looking

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
YOUR PROJECT STRUCTURE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

network-automation/
├─ backend/
│  └─ vpn_automation/
│     ├─ discovery.py
│     ├─ client.py
│     └─ build.py
��─ requirements.txt
├─ .github/
│  └─ workflows/
│     └─ vpn-automation.yml

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WORKFLOW WITHOUT ACTIONS (PROBLEM)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

name: VPN Automation Test

on: push

jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:

      # PROBLEM: Manual code download
      - name: Clone repository
        run: git clone https://github.com/MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation.git
        # You have to write git command
        # You handle errors
        # Complex

      # PROBLEM: Manual Python setup
      - name: Update system packages
        run: sudo apt-get update
        # You manage system
        # You write commands
        # Error prone

      - name: Install Python
        run: sudo apt-get install python3.9 python3-pip
        # You configure everything
        # Takes multiple lines
        # Hard to read

      - name: Create virtual environment
        run: python3.9 -m venv venv && source venv/bin/activate
        # You manage venv
        # Complex command
        # Easy to make mistakes

      # PROBLEM: Manual dependency install
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: pip install -r requirements.txt
        # This works but complex setup needed

      - name: Run discovery
        run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py
        # Code might not be there!
        # discovery.py not found (code not cloned yet)

      - name: Run client
        run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py
        # Needs network_data.json from discovery
        # Error handling?

      - name: Run build
        run: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py
        # Everything depends on previous steps
        # If one fails, all affected

Issues:
   ├─ 50+ lines of code
   ├─ Complex commands
   ├─ Hard to understand
   ├─ Not maintainable
   ├─ Easy to break
   ├─ Lots of manual work
   └─ Not professional

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WORKFLOW WITH ACTIONS (SOLUTION)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

name: VPN Automation Test

on: push

jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:

      # SOLUTION 1: GitHub handles code download
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
        # Official GitHub action
        # Downloads code automatically
        # Error handling built-in
        # 1 line!
        # Now code is on runner

      # SOLUTION 2: GitHub handles Python setup
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v4
        with:
          python-version: '3.9'
        # Official GitHub action
        # Installs Python 3.9
        # Configures pip
        # Error handling built-in
        # 3 lines!
        # Python is ready

      # SOLUTION 3: Install dependencies
      - run: pip install -r requirements.txt
        # Now simple because Python ready
        # Dependencies installed
        # All packages available

      # SOLUTION 4: Run your scripts
      - run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py
        # Code exists (checkout did it)
        # Packages exist (requirements installed)
        # Runs successfully

      - run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py
        # Code exists
        # network_data.json from previous step
        # Runs successfully

      - run: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py
        # Everything available
        # All dependencies satisfied
        # Builds successfully

Benefits:
   ├─ ~20 lines of code (vs 50+)
   ├─ Simple to understand
   ├─ Easy to maintain
   ├─ Professional
   ├─ Reliable
   ├─ GitHub handles complexity
   └─ Clear intent

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
DETAILED BREAKDOWN - WHAT EACH PART DOES
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

STEP 1: CHECKOUT ACTION

- uses: actions/checkout@v3
  # What it does:
  # ├─ GitHub official action
  # ├─ Downloads network-automation code
  # ├─ Places it on runner
  # ├─ Sets up Git
  # ├─ Handles all errors
  # └─ Takes 30 seconds

  # Why needed:
  # ├─ Runner doesn't have code
  # ├─ Repository is storage
  # ├─ Runner is execution machine
  # └─ Must download code first

  # Result:
  # ├─ /backend folder available
  # ├─ /discovery.py accessible
  # ├─ requirements.txt available
  # └─ Everything ready ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

STEP 2: SETUP-PYTHON ACTION

- uses: actions/setup-python@v4
  with:
    python-version: '3.9'
  # What it does:
  # ├─ GitHub official action
  # ├─ Installs Python 3.9
  # ├─ Installs pip
  # ├─ Configures environment
  # ├─ Handles all setup
  # └─ Takes 20 seconds

  # Why needed:
  # ├─ Runner has no Python by default
  # ├─ Need Python 3.9 specifically
  # ├─ Need pip for packages
  # └─ Action handles all setup

  # Result:
  # ├─ Python 3.9 installed ✓
  # ├─ pip available ✓
  # ├─ Environment variables set ✓
  # └─ Ready for pip commands ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

STEP 3: INSTALL DEPENDENCIES

- run: pip install -r requirements.txt
  # What it does:
  # ├─ Simple pip command
  # ├─ Reads requirements.txt
  # ├─ Installs all packages
  # └─ Takes 30 seconds

  # Why possible:
  # ├─ Python 3.9 already installed
  # ├─ pip already available
  # ├─ requirements.txt on runner
  # └─ All prerequisites met

  # Packages installed:
  # ├─ requests
  # ├─ paramiko
  # ├─ netaddr
  # └─ Others from requirements.txt

  # Result:
  # ├─ All packages installed ✓
  # ├─ Available for import ✓
  # └─ Ready for scripts ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

STEP 4: DISCOVERY SCRIPT

- run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py
  # What it does:
  # ├─ Runs discovery script
  # ├─ Scans networks
  # ├─ Discovers devices
  # ├─ Creates network_data.json
  # └─ Takes 10 seconds

  # Why it works:
  # ├─ Code exists (checkout done)
  # ├─ Python 3.9 available
  # ├─ All packages installed
  # ├─ File discoverable
  # └─ All prerequisites met

  # Result:
  # ├─ Network scan completed ✓
  # ├─ network_data.json created ✓
  # ├─ Data saved on runner ✓
  # └─ Ready for next step ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

STEP 5: CLIENT SCRIPT

- run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py
  # What it does:
  # ├─ Runs VPN client script
  # ├─ Reads network_data.json
  # ├─ Connects to VPN
  # ├─ Creates connection logs
  # └─ Takes 15 seconds

  # Why it works:
  # ├─ Code exists
  # ├─ Python available
  # ├─ Packages installed
  # ├─ network_data.json from step 4
  # ├─ Same runner (data preserved)
  # └─ All prerequisites met

  # KEY POINT:
  # └─ SAME RUNNER = Files preserved!
  #    Without actions (separate steps):
  #    ├─ Step 4: Create file in Environment A
  #    ├─ Step 5: Fresh Environment B
  #    ├─ File lost! ✗
  #    With actions (same runner):
  #    ├─ Step 4: Create file
  #    ├─ Step 5: Same environment
  #    ├─ File available! ✓

  # Result:
  # ├─ VPN connection successful ✓
  # ├─ Connection logs created ✓
  # ├─ Ready for next step ✓
  # └─ All data preserved ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━���━

STEP 6: BUILD SCRIPT

- run: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py
  # What it does:
  # ├─ Runs build script
  # ├─ Uses data from discovery
  # ├─ Uses data from client
  # ├─ Packages everything
  # ├─ Creates deployment
  # └─ Takes 5 seconds

  # Why it works:
  # ├─ All code exists
  # ├─ All packages available
  # ├─ All data from previous steps
  # ├─ network_data.json available
  # ├─ Connection logs available
  # └─ Everything ready

  # Result:
  # ├─ Build completed ✓
  # ├─ Deployment package created ✓
  # ├─ Ready to deploy ✓
  # └─ Workflow successful! ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
EXECUTION FLOW
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

You push code to GitHub:
   $ git push origin main

GitHub detects push → Workflow triggers! 🚀

Runner allocated (Ubuntu machine):
   ├─ Empty machine
   ├─ No code
   ├─ No Python
   └─ Ready to execute

Step 1: actions/checkout@v3
   ├─ Downloads network-automation code
   ├─ Code now on runner
   ├─ discovery.py available
   ├─ requirements.txt available
   └─ Status: ✓ Complete

Step 2: actions/setup-python@v4
   ├─ Installs Python 3.9
   ├─ Configures pip
   ├─ Sets environment variables
   └─ Status: ✓ Complete

Step 3: pip install
   ├─ Reads requirements.txt
   ├─ Installs requests package
   ├─ Installs paramiko package
   ├─ Installs netaddr package
   └─ Status: ✓ Complete

Step 4: discovery.py
   ├─ Runs discovery script
   ├─ Scans networks
   ├─ Creates network_data.json
   └─ Status: ✓ Complete

Step 5: client.py
   ├─ Reads network_data.json
   ├─ Connects to VPN
   ├─ Creates logs
   └─ Status: ✓ Complete

Step 6: build.py
   ├─ Uses all previous data
   ├─ Packages everything
   ├─ Creates deployment
   └─ Status: ✓ Complete

All steps successful!
Workflow complete! ✓ 🚀

Results visible in GitHub Actions tab

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━���━

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
