43. Checking Out Code In Workflows
-----------------------------------
HOW TO USE ACTIONS - CHECKOUT ACTION SYNTAX

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
USING ACTIONS IN WORKFLOW
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Instead of RUN keyword:
   └─ run: command

Use USES keyword:
   └─ uses: action-identifier

Keyword Purpose:

run: ← For shell commands
   └─ Executes terminal commands

uses: ← For GitHub actions
   └─ Uses pre-built actions
   └─ GitHub or community maintained

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
CHECKOUT ACTION SYNTAX (Image 4)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Basic syntax:

steps:
  - name: Get code
    uses: actions/checkout@v3

Breaking it down:

- name: Get code
  # Human-readable step name
  # Shows in Actions tab
  # Describes what step does

uses: actions/checkout@v3
  # uses = Use an action
  # actions = GitHub organization
  # checkout = Action name
  # @v3 = Version number

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
ACTION IDENTIFIER
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Format:

actions/checkout@v3
  │       │        │
  │       │        └─ Version (@v3)
  │       └─ Action name (checkout)
  └─ Organization (actions)

What is this identifier?

actions/checkout
   └─ GitHub repository path
   └─ https://github.com/actions/checkout
   └─ Where action code lives

How to find identifier?

1. Go to Marketplace
   └─ github.com/marketplace

2. Search for action
   └─ Example: "checkout"

3. View action page
   └─ Find usage section
   └─ Copy identifier

4. Use in workflow
   └─ uses: actions/checkout@v3

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
VERSION SPECIFICATION (@v3)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━��━

Why specify version?

Actions get updated:
   ├─ New features added
   ├─ Bug fixes made
   ├─ Breaking changes possible
   └─ Behavior might change

Problem without version lock:

uses: actions/checkout
   # Always uses latest version
   # Latest version might break your workflow
   # Unexpected behavior possible
   # Not predictable

Solution with version lock:

uses: actions/checkout@v3
   # Always uses v3
   # Predictable behavior
   # No unexpected changes
   # Safe and stable

Available versions:

To find latest version:

Option 1: Check Marketplace
   └─ github.com/marketplace/actions/checkout
   └─ See available versions

Option 2: Check Repository
   └─ github.com/actions/checkout
   └─ Go to Releases tab
   └─ See all versions

Example versions:

@v3 ← Latest (at time of recording)
@v2 ← Older version
@main ← Development version (not recommended)

How to choose version?

Recommendation:
   └─ Use latest stable version
   └─ At time of recording: v3
   └─ Test before updating to newer version

Current status:

When you watch this:
   └─ Might have v4, v5, etc.
   └─ Use latest available
   └─ Should still work backward compatible

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WITH KEYWORD FOR ACTION CONFIGURATION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Some actions need configuration:

Syntax with configuration:

uses: action-name@version
with:
  option-key: option-value
  another-key: another-value

What is "with"?

with:
   ├─ Keyword for action configuration
   ├─ Provides input to action
   ├─ Action uses these values
   ├─ Changes action behavior
   └─ Optional (not always needed)

Example: Setup Python action

uses: actions/setup-python@v4
with:
  python-version: '3.9'
  # with = configuration keyword
  # python-version = configuration key
  # '3.9' = configuration value

How action uses "with":

Setup-python action:
   ├─ Reads python-version: '3.9'
   ├─ Installs Python 3.9
   ├─ Not 3.8, not 3.10
   ├─ Exactly 3.9
   └─ Because of "with" configuration

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
CHECKOUT ACTION - WITH CONFIGURATION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Checkout action options (with keyword):

uses: actions/checkout@v3
with:
  repository: owner/repo
  # Which repository to clone
  # Default: Current repository
  # Optional

with:
  ref: branch-name
  # Which branch/tag/commit
  # Default: Default branch (main)
  # Optional

with:
  path: ./checkout-path
  # Where to clone code
  # Default: Current directory
  # Optional

with:
  fetch-depth: 0
  # How much history to fetch
  # Default: 1 (minimal)
  # Optional

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
CHECKOUT ACTION - DEFAULT BEHAVIOR
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Default usage (no "with"):

uses: actions/checkout@v3

Default behavior:

Repository:
   └─ Uses current repository
   └─ Where workflow file is located
   └─ Your repository automatically

Branch:
   └─ Uses default branch
   └─ Usually "main" or "master"
   └─ No need to specify

Path:
   └─ Clones to current directory
   └─ Root of runner's workspace
   └─ Code available everywhere

Result:

Your code downloaded to runner:
   ├─ /backend folder available
   ├─ /src folder available
   ├─ package.json available
   ├─ All files in workspace
   └─ Ready to use!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHEN TO USE "WITH" CONFIGURATION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

For checkout action:

Most cases (don't need "with"):

uses: actions/checkout@v3

Advanced cases (need "with"):

Clone different repository:
   uses: actions/checkout@v3
   with:
     repository: other-org/other-repo

Clone specific branch:
   uses: actions/checkout@v3
   with:
     ref: develop

Clone to specific path:
   uses: actions/checkout@v3
   with:
     path: ./my-code

Multiple repositories:

steps:
  - uses: actions/checkout@v3
    with:
      path: ./repo1

  - uses: actions/checkout@v3
    with:
      repository: other-org/repo2
      path: ./repo2

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
YOUR VPN PROJECT EXAMPLE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Simple checkout for your project:

steps:
  - name: Get code
    uses: actions/checkout@v3
    # Uses default settings
    # Clones network-automation repo
    # To current directory
    # Ready for next steps

With custom configuration:

steps:
  - name: Get code
    uses: actions/checkout@v3
    with:
      repository: MANISH-GANVIR/network-automation
      ref: main
      path: ./app
    # Explicitly specify repository
    # Explicitly specify branch
    # Clone to ./app directory

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FINDING DOCUMENTATION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Where to find action documentation:

Marketplace page:
   └─ github.com/marketplace/actions/checkout
   └─ Usage examples
   └─ Configuration options
   └─ Best practices

Repository page:
   └─ github.com/actions/checkout
   └─ README.md
   └─ Detailed documentation
   └─ Latest version info

Official documentation:
   └─ docs.github.com
   └─ Search: "GitHub Actions"
   └─ Complete reference
   └─ All available actions

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SUMMARY - HOW TO USE ACTIONS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Step 1: Use "uses" keyword
   └─ uses: action-identifier@version

Step 2: Find action identifier
   └─ Check Marketplace
   └─ Copy from documentation

Step 3: Specify version
   └─ Lock version with @v3
   └─ Prevents unexpected changes
   └─ Ensures predictability

Step 4: Add configuration (if needed)
   └─ Use "with" keyword
   └─ Provide required options
   └─ Most actions have defaults

Step 5: Check documentation
   └─ Understand what action does
   └─ Learn available options
   └─ Follow best practices

For checkout action:

Minimal (most common):

- uses: actions/checkout@v3

Advanced (custom config):

- uses: actions/checkout@v3
  with:
    repository: owner/repo
    ref: branch
    path: ./path

Done! Now you can use actions! 🚀
============================================
44. Configuring Actions:-
------------------------
SETUP NODE.JS ACTION - SHORT GUIDE (ENGLISH)

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT IS A RUNNER?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Runner = Virtual machine provided by GitHub

What it does:
   ├─ Executes workflow steps
   ├─ Has operating system (Linux/Windows/Mac)
   ├─ Has pre-installed software
   ├─ Temporary (created for each workflow)
   ├─ Destroyed after workflow ends
   └─ Free for public repos

Your runner:

   runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   ├─ Linux machine
   ├─ Ubuntu OS
   ├─ Latest version
   ├─ Free tier
   └─ Suitable for most projects

What's pre-installed on ubuntu-latest?

Pre-installed software:
   ├─ Node.js (v16, v18, etc.)
   ├─ Python
   ├─ Git
   ├─ Docker
   ├─ npm (Node Package Manager)
   ├─ pip (Python Package Manager)
   └─ Many other tools

Where to check what's available:
   └─ docs.github.com → Search "Supported Software"

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHAT IS NPM?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

npm = Node Package Manager

Purpose:

Tool to manage JavaScript dependencies
   ├─ Install packages (libraries)
   ├─ Manage versions
   ├─ Run scripts
   └─ Build projects

Comes with Node.js:

When you install Node.js:
   └─ npm automatically installed
   └─ No separate installation needed

What npm does:

# Install dependencies from package.json
$ npm install

# Run test script
$ npm test

# Run development server
$ npm run dev

# Install specific package
$ npm install axios

Package.json file:

Contains:
   ├─ Project name
   ├─ Version
   ├─ Dependencies (packages needed)
   ├─ Scripts (commands to run)
   └─ Other metadata

Example:

{
  "name": "react-demo",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "dependencies": {
    "react": "^18.0.0",
    "axios": "^1.4.0"
  },
  "scripts": {
    "install": "npm install",
    "test": "jest",
    "dev": "vite"
  }
}

When you run npm install:
   ├─ Reads package.json
   ├─ Downloads all dependencies
   ├─ Creates node_modules folder
   ├─ Creates package-lock.json
   └─ All packages available

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PREINSTALLED SOFTWARE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

ubuntu-latest comes with:
   ├─ Node.js preinstalled ✓
   ├─ npm preinstalled ✓ (comes with Node.js)
   ├─ Python preinstalled ✓
   └─ Many more tools

But what if:

You need different version?
   ├─ Preinstalled: Node.js v16
   ├─ You want: Node.js v18
   └─ Solution: Use setup-node action

You want explicit setup?
   ├─ For clarity
   ├─ For documentation
   ├─ For future compatibility
   └─ Solution: Use setup-node action

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SETUP-NODE ACTION (Image 5)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Purpose:

Install and configure Node.js on runner

Syntax:

- name: Install Node.js
  uses: actions/setup-node@v3
  with:
    node-version: '18'
    # npm comes automatically with Node.js

What it does:
   ├─ Installs Node.js v18
   ├─ Automatically installs npm with it
   ├─ Configures environment variables
   ├─ Sets up PATH for npm commands
   └─ Ready for npm install and npm test

Why use it:

Even if preinstalled:
   ├─ Specify exact version (v18, not v16)
   ├─ Clear documentation
   ├─ Best practice
   ├─ Future compatibility
   └─ Professional workflow

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
YOUR WORKFLOW SO FAR
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

steps:

  - name: Get code
    uses: actions/checkout@v3
    # Downloads code from GitHub repository
    # Code now available on runner

  - name: Install Node.js
    uses: actions/setup-node@v3
    with:
      node-version: '18'
    # Installs Node.js v18
    # npm (Node Package Manager) automatically installed
    # Ready to use npm commands

Next steps to add:
   ├─ npm install
   #  └─ Install dependencies from package.json
   #     npm reads package.json
   #     Downloads all required packages
   #     Creates node_modules folder
   │
   └─ npm test
      └─ Run automated tests
         npm runs test script defined in package.json
         Reports test results

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
YOUR VPN PROJECT EQUIVALENT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

steps:

  - uses: actions/checkout@v3
    # Get code from GitHub

  - uses: actions/setup-python@v4
    with:
      python-version: '3.9'
    # Install Python v3.9
    # pip (Python Package Manager) automatically included

  - run: pip install -r requirements.txt
    # Install Python packages (like npm install for Node.js)
    # pip reads requirements.txt
    # Downloads all Python packages

  - run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py
    # Run your Python script
    # All dependencies available

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FLOW COMPARISON
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

REACT PROJECT (Node.js):

Step 1: Checkout
   └─ Get code

Step 2: Setup-node
   ├─ Install Node.js
   └─ npm available (comes with Node.js)

Step 3: npm install
   └─ Install dependencies from package.json

Step 4: npm test
   └─ Run tests

VPN PROJECT (Python):

Step 1: Checkout
   └─ Get code

Step 2: Setup-python
   ├─ Install Python
   └─ pip available (comes with Python)

Step 3: pip install
   └─ Install dependencies from requirements.txt

Step 4: python script.py
   └─ Run script

Both follow same pattern:
   ├─ Get code
   ├─ Setup language tool
   ├─ Install dependencies
   └─ Run your code

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
IMAGE 5 CODE vs GUIDE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Image 5 shows:

- name: Get code
  uses: actions/checkout@v3

- name: Install Node.js
  uses: actions/setup-node@v3
  with:
    node-version: 18

This guide contains:

- name: Get code
  uses: actions/checkout@v3
  # Downloads code from GitHub

- name: Install Node.js
  uses: actions/setup-node@v3
  with:
    node-version: '18'
    # npm comes automatically with Node.js

Match status: ✓ COMPLETE MATCH!

Minor difference: 18 vs '18'
   └─ Both are exactly the same
   └─ YAML accepts both formats

Conclusion:
   └─ All code from Image 5 is in this guide! ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Runner = Virtual machine from GitHub
   └─ Executes your workflow steps
   └─ Pre-installed with many tools

npm = Node Package Manager
   └─ Tool for managing JavaScript packages
   └─ Comes automatically with Node.js
   └─ Used to install dependencies
   └─ Used to run scripts

Workflow Pattern:

Step 1: Checkout
   └─ Get code from repository

Step 2: Setup language
   └─ Install Node.js (setup-node)
   └─ Or Python (setup-python)

Step 3: Install dependencies
   └─ npm install (for Node.js)
   └─ pip install (for Python)

Step 4: Run your code
   └─ npm test
   └─ python script.py

Status: ✓ Ready for next step! 🚀
========================================
45. Adding More Workflow Job Steps
INSTALLING DEPENDENCIES & RUNNING TESTS - SHORT

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 3: INSTALL DEPENDENCIES
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

- run: npm ci

What is npm ci?

npm ci = npm clean install
   ├─ Install from package-lock.json
   ├─ Exact same versions
   ├─ Safe for CI/CD
   └─ Better than npm install

Why npm ci?

npm ci:
   ├─ Uses locked versions
   ├─ Predictable
   ├─ No breaking changes
   └─ Professional choice

npm install:
   └─ May install newer versions
   └─ Less predictable

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
STEP 4: RUN TESTS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

- run: npm test

What it does:
   ├─ Executes all tests
   ├─ Runs .test.jsx files
   ├─ Reports results
   └─ Pass or fail

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
COMPLETE WORKFLOW
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

name: Test Project

on: push

jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:

      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
        # Get code

      - uses: actions/setup-node@v3
        with:
          node-version: '18'
        # Install Node.js & npm

      - run: npm ci
        # Install dependencies

      - run: npm test
        # Run tests

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PUSH TO GITHUB
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

$ git add .github/workflows/test.yml
$ git commit -m "Added test workflow"
$ git push origin main

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
TOKEN ERROR
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Error:
   └─ "Personal access token has no workflow permission"

Solution:

1. Go to github.com/settings
2. Developer settings → Personal access tokens
3. Generate new token
4. Select scopes:
   ├─ repo
   └─ workflow ← IMPORTANT
5. Copy token
6. Update git credentials with new token
7. Push again

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
AUTOMATIC EXECUTION
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

When you push:
   ├─ Workflow triggers automatically 🚀
   ├─ Runner allocated
   ├─ All 4 steps execute
   ├─ Tests run
   └─ Results in Actions tab

View results:

GitHub → Actions tab → Select workflow
   ├─ View each step
   ├─ View test results
   └─ Debug if needed

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
YOUR VPN PROJECT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

steps:

  - uses: actions/checkout@v3

  - uses: actions/setup-python@v4
    with:
      python-version: '3.9'

  - run: pip install -r requirements.txt

  - run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py

  - run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py

  - run: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

npm ci → Install dependencies (safe)
npm test → Run tests

Push workflow → GitHub
   ├─ May need new token
   └─ If error: Add "workflow" scope

Workflow executes automatically
   └─ Results in Actions tab

Done! 🚀
=======================================================
46. Failing Workflows & Analyzing Workflows
-------------------------------------------
FAILING WORKFLOWS - SIMPLE & SHORT

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WHEN WORKFLOWS FAIL?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Workflows fail when:
   ├─ Tests fail
   ├─ Code errors
   ├─ Commands error
   └─ Dependencies missing

Good thing:
   └─ Catches bugs before production! ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
BREAKING A TEST (EXAMPLE)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Original code:

expect(heading).toBeInTheDocument();

Break it:

expect(heading).not.toBeInTheDocument();
                └─ Added .not = test fails

Commit & push:

$ git add src/MainContent.test.jsx
$ git commit -m "Changed test"
$ git push origin main

Result:
   ├─ Workflow triggers 🚀
   ├─ npm test runs
   └─ Test FAILS ✗

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
VIEWING FAILED WORKFLOW
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

GitHub Actions tab:

1. Click failed workflow
2. Click failed step (Run tests)
3. See detailed error log
4. Understand what failed

Error shows:
   ├─ Expected: NOT in document
   ├─ Received: IN document
   └─ Test failed! ✗

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FIXING THE ISSUE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Remove the .not:

expect(heading).toBeInTheDocument();

Commit & push:

$ git add src/MainContent.test.jsx
$ git commit -m "Fixed test"
$ git push origin main

Workflow runs again:
   ├─ npm test runs
   └─ Test PASSES ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
USING GIT REVERT (CLEANER)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Instead of manual fix:

$ git log
# Find commit ID (e.g., abc123de)

$ git revert abc123de
# Creates new commit that undoes old commit

$ git push origin main
# Push new revert commit

Result:
   ├─ Old commit: Added .not
   ├─ New commit: Reverted it
   ├─ Clean history ✓
   └─ Workflow passes ✓

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
WORKFLOW AFTER FIX
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

New push triggers workflow:

Step 1: Get code ✓
Step 2: Setup Node.js ✓
Step 3: npm ci ✓
Step 4: npm test ✓ SUCCESS!

Final status:
   └─ ✓ All tests pass
   └─ ✓ Workflow successful
   └─ ✓ Green badge

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
KEY POINTS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

1. Failing tests catch bugs early
2. GitHub shows detailed logs
3. Analyze errors in Actions tab
4. Fix code locally
5. Push fix → Workflow runs again
6. Verify success in Actions tab

Done! 🚀
======================================================
47. Adding Multiple Jobs IN A WORKFLOW

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PROBLEM
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Scenario:

Need to:
   ├─ Test code
   ├─ Build project
   └─ Deploy application

Current workflow:
   └─ Only 1 job (test)
   └─ Can't do all tasks
   └─ Takes long time for multiple tasks

Issue:
   ├─ Sequential execution slow
   ├─ Can't build & test together
   ├─ Inefficient workflow
   └─ All tasks mixed in one job

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
SOLUTION: MULTIPLE JOBS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Add second job to workflow:

name: Deploy Project

on: push

jobs:

  test:                    # Job 1
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v3
        with:
          node-version: '18'
      - run: npm ci
      - run: npm test       # Test only

  deploy:                  # Job 2
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v3
        with:
          node-version: '18'
      - run: npm ci
      - run: npm run build  # Build only
      - run: echo "Deploying"

Benefits:

✓ Two separate jobs
✓ Each has own responsibility
✓ Run in parallel (faster!)
✓ Cleaner code
✓ Professional workflow

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
KEY POINTS
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

1. Jobs on same indent level:

jobs:
  test:      # Same level
  deploy:    # Same level

2. Each job needs:
   ├─ runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   ├─ steps:
   └─ At least one step

3. Default: Jobs run in PARALLEL

   Timeline:
   test:   [====]
   deploy: [====]
           (same time)

4. Not sum of time:
   ├─ test: 30 seconds
   ├─ deploy: 20 seconds
   ├─ Total: 35 seconds (not 50!)
   └─ They overlap

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━��━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PUSH & EXECUTE
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

$ git add .github/workflows/deploy.yml
$ git commit -m "Added deploy job"
$ git push origin main

Result:
   ├─ Workflow triggers 🚀
   ├─ 2 jobs run in parallel
   ├─ Both complete together
   └─ Results in Actions tab

GitHub Actions tab shows:

test ✓ Passed
deploy ✓ Passed
Workflow ✓ Successful

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
YOUR VPN PROJECT
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

name: VPN Automation

on: push

jobs:

  discovery:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v4
        with:
          python-version: '3.9'
      - run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/discovery.py

  client:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v4
        with:
          python-version: '3.9'
      - run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - run: python backend/vpn_automation/asa/client.py

  build:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v4
        with:
          python-version: '3.9'
      - run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - run: python backend/vpn_automation/build.py

3 jobs run together! ✓

SUMMARY
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Problem:
   └─ Single job can't handle multiple tasks

Solution:
   └─ Add multiple jobs to workflow
   └─ Each job separate responsibility
   └─ Jobs run in parallel

Benefits:
   ├─ Faster execution
   ├─ Cleaner code
   ├─ Professional
   ├─ Scalable
   └─ Easy to manage

Rules:
   ├─ Jobs on same indent
   ├─ Each job: runs-on + steps
   ├─ Run in parallel by default
   └─ YAML indentation critical

Done! 🚀
